# Research log

Dated history behind the current state documented in `CLAUDE.md`: corrections, rejected approaches, session-by-session implementation notes, bugs found and fixed, diagnostic dead ends. `CLAUDE.md` states only where things currently stand; this file is the trail of *how* we got there and *why* alternatives were rejected. Not auto-loaded into every session — read on demand when picking up a specific thread or needing the reasoning behind a current decision.

Organized by topic, chronological within each topic.

## Notation history

**Notation trap, why it mattered.** The approved first-stage paper (`56-id-evasion.qmd`, footnote `linear-exp`) defines its own "$e$" *multiplicatively* ($M^*=M\exp(e)$) — that paper's $e$ **is** this deck's $u$, not the additive structural $e$ used in $q(e)$, $\kappa(e,\omega)$. Deconvolution only ever recovers $u=\ln(1+e/M)$, never the level $e$ — a property of the current CD/log-linear identification strategy specifically (under translog the map would need rederiving, though the general principle survives: whatever enters the log residual $\mathcal V=u-\varepsilon$ additively is some transform of $e$ scaled by $M$). Previously kept $\tilde e$ for continuity with the paper.

**Notation resolved (2026-07-28).** As of this date, switched notation to $u$ throughout the Quarto-Slides deck so readers don't mistake the overreporting log-ratio for the structural level $e$ — the approved paper (`56-id-evasion.qmd`) is untouched/read-only and still uses its own $e$ for this object, so translate when citing it. All four Quarto-Slides files that used this symbol (`200-model.qmd`, `600-opt-tax.qmd`, `500-opt-tax-claude.qmd`, `700-deconvolving-evasion.qmd`) now consistently use $u$ — previously $\tilde e$, and previously inconsistently mixed with $u$ inside `700-deconvolving-evasion.qmd` specifically.

## First-stage / density-transformation corrections

**Jacobian correction (2026-07-30).** Earlier version of the Jacobian line read $J_i(\varepsilon)=|h'(e)|(M^*-e)$, from differentiating $u=\ln(M^*/(M^*-e))$ holding $M^*$ fixed. That's a partial/total-derivative error — $M^*=M+e$ is not fixed as $e$ varies (it's the thing that moves), so that derivative silently assumed $\partial M^*/\partial e=0$. $M$ (from the production-side materials FOC, independent of $e$) is the quantity actually fixed. The value of $u_{it}$ itself is unaffected (both formulas give $\ln(M^*_{it}/M_{it})$); only the Jacobian's functional form changes, by a factor of $M^*/M$. Corrected to $J_i(\varepsilon)=|h'(e)|\,M^*$. Full worked correction in `Paper/sections/9999-density-trans.qmd`.

**Correction (2026-08-12): $\omega\perp(K,L)$ was silently assumed and is untenable.** The bare $f_\omega(\omega(\varepsilon))$ in the unconditional likelihood silently assumed $\omega\perp(K,L)$ — untenable, since $\omega$ is AR(1)-persistent and $K$ is a dynamically accumulated state chosen anticipating that persistence; asserting independence would strip the AR(1) moment conditions (`eq-eta-moments` in `56-id-evasion.qmd`) of the instrument relevance that identifies $\alpha_K,\alpha_L$ in the first place. Fix: Markov timing ($K,L$ predetermined; the AR(1) innovation $\eta_{it}$ is news independent of everything dated $t-1$) gives $\omega_{it}\perp(K_{it},L_{it})\mid\omega_{it-1}$ — $\omega_{it-1}$ is a sufficient statistic for $(K,L)$'s relevance, so the likelihood should condition on the firm's own lagged $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-1}$ instead of $(K,L)$, replacing bare $f_\omega$ with the AR(1) innovation density $f_\eta$ and marginalizing the one remaining unknown $\varepsilon_{it-1}$ (via $\omega_{it-1}=\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-1}-(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{it-1}$) — a 2-D integral over $(\varepsilon_{it},\varepsilon_{it-1})$, collapsing back to the static $\mathcal L_i^{\text{unc}}$ exactly when the AR(1) has no persistence. $f_\eta$ itself is recoverable by Fourier deconvolution from `56-id-evasion.qmd`'s AR(1) regression residual (`eq-big-w-reg`), reusing stage-1's $f_\varepsilon$. **Notation clash:** `56-id-evasion.qmd`'s AR(1) drift is also called $h$ (linear case $\delta_0+\delta_1\xi$) — collides with both the MSL doc's $h(e)$ and $\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2$; renamed locally to $g(\xi)=\gamma_0+\gamma_1\xi$ wherever the two are combined. Full derivation, and the correction to an earlier draft claim that the bare unconditional likelihood already sufficed, in `Paper/sections/9999-density-trans.qmd`. **Left genuinely unresolved at the time:** a firm's first-observed period has no $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-1}$ to condition on (dynamic-panel initial-conditions problem). This whole MSL/quadrature dynamic-likelihood strand was subsequently superseded in practice by the ELVIS moment-based stage-2 approach (see below), which never needs $f_\eta$ or $f_\varepsilon$ at all — so this correction remains a valid derivation (kept in `9999-density-trans.qmd`) but is no longer on the critical path for estimation.

**$\varepsilon$ is measurement error, not an output shock (confirmed by Hans, 2026-09-24).** The supervisors proposed treating $\varepsilon$ as measurement error in output with $E[\varepsilon]=0$, instead of the ex-post output shock of GNR (2020). That is why productivity is $\exp(\omega)$ rather than the canonical $\exp(\omega+\varepsilon)$ (Olley–Pakes onward), and why the first stage in use (`first_stage_panel_me`, feeding ch. 6/8) sets $\mathcal E=1$: with measurement error the firm's FOC has no $E[\exp\varepsilon]$ term, so $\hat\beta=\exp(E[s\mid\text{corp}])$. The approved `56-id-evasion.qmd` predates this and keeps GNR's $\mathcal E$; `first_stage_panel` (feeding the test/deconvolution) also still computes $\mathcal E$, but only $\ln D=E[s\mid\text{corp}]$ enters $\mathcal V$, so test and deconvolution results are identical under either convention. Caught while porting ch. 4 (the ported $\mathcal E$ equations conflicted with ch. 2's stated $E[\exp\varepsilon]=1$, which was itself a misstatement; CLAUDE.md said the same and is corrected).

## Estimation method selection: MSM rejected, ELVIS adopted

**MSM — investigated and rejected (2026-07-28).** The natural-looking alternative — simulating $\psi(\varepsilon^{(s)})$ (or $M^{(s)}=\beta P_tY_{it}e^{-\varepsilon^{(s)}}/\rho_t$, $e^{(s)}=M^*-M^{(s)}$, algebraically **identical** to the $\mathcal V$-based construction since $\textbf{e}^{-\mathcal V_{it}}=\beta P_tY_{it}/(\rho_tM^*_{it})$ makes them the same map) and averaging, or wrapping that average in classic GMM with instruments — does **not** deliver a valid moment: $\psi(\cdot)$ is nonlinear in $\varepsilon$, so the simulated average converges to $\int\psi(\varepsilon;\theta)f_\varepsilon(\varepsilon)d\varepsilon$, not to the firm's true $\psi_{it}=\psi(\varepsilon_{it};\theta)$ — it's missing exactly the posterior reweighting $f_\psi(\psi(\varepsilon))J_i(\varepsilon)f_\omega(\omega(\varepsilon))$ that makes MSL's analogous simulated average valid (MSL never averages $\psi(\varepsilon^{(s)})$ itself, only the reweighted density). GMM's instruments/weighting-matrix machinery only combines already-valid moments efficiently; it cannot fix an invalid one. A valid simulated-moments design would instead match a data-computable, $\psi$-free population object (e.g. $E[\mathcal V_{it}|Z]=E[u_{it}|Z]$) against a forward-simulated analog from trial $(\psi,\omega)$ draws — ties to stage 1's deconvolved $f_u$ — but delivers $\theta$ not $f_\psi$ and adds machinery for no real gain over quadrature-MLE. See `600-opt-tax.qmd` for the full explainer (kept as a "why not" section).

**ELVIS — investigated and adopted as a genuine third alternative (2026-08-19).** Schennach (2014, *Econometrica*, "Entropic Latent Variable Integration via Simulation," `@Schennach2014` in `biblio/b100422.bib`) solves exactly the failure mode identified in the MSM rejection above — a moment function nonlinear in an unobservable — without the naive-averaging bug. Recast the moment in the **true materials level** $M_{it}$ as the one unobservable (mirroring Schennach's own canonical errors-in-variables example, her Example 1.5, unobserved $X^*$ vs. observed $X=X^*+\Delta X$): $e(M)=M^*_{it}-M$, $\varepsilon(M)=\ln(M^*_{it}/M)-\mathcal V_{it}$, $\omega(M)=\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}-(1-\beta)\varepsilon(M)$, $g(M,Z_{it},\theta)\equiv\psi(M)=h(e(M))-\delta_0+\delta_1\omega(M)-\delta_2\omega(M)^2$, support $\mathcal U_{Z,\theta}=(M^*_{it}-\tfrac1{2\lambda},M^*_{it})$ ($\theta$-dependent support is explicitly covered, her §4.1). Her Theorem 2.1: the infinite-dimensional question "does *some* conditional distribution of $M$ given $Z$ satisfy $E[g(M,Z,\theta)]=0$" is **equivalent** to a finite-dimensional system in $(\theta,\gamma)$ built from an entropy-maximizing **exponential tilt** $\exp(\gamma'g(M,Z,\theta))$ of a user-chosen dominating measure $\rho(M|Z)$ — solved via simulated draws $M^{(s)}\sim\rho(\cdot|Z)$ and a ratio-of-averages $\tilde g(Z,\theta,\gamma)=E_\rho[g\cdot e^{\gamma'g}]/E_\rho[e^{\gamma'g}]$, fed into standard GMM/EL over $(\theta,\gamma)$ jointly. **This tilt is precisely the missing posterior reweighting** that the rejected naive-MSM average lacked — not assumed, but solved for as a Lagrange multiplier on the moment constraint (her §2.2 derivation). Two practical payoffs: (i) doesn't need $f_\psi$ (same limit as MSM, expected), but (ii) **doesn't need $f_\varepsilon$ either** — by her Remark 2.3, the choice of $\rho$ has *zero effect* on the point estimate (only support matters), so stage 1's deconvolution of $f_\varepsilon$/$f_u$ becomes optional for stage 2 estimation of $\theta$ (only $\hat\beta$ is needed); $\hat f_\varepsilon$ can still be reused as an efficient $\rho$ to cut simulation variance, but that's a computational choice, not an identification requirement. Delivers $\theta=(\lambda,\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2)$ only, not $f_\psi$ — same trade-off as MSM, just valid. Needs $\ge4$ instrument-interacted moments for point identification (order condition); under-identification degrades gracefully to a set estimate. Identification of $\lambda$ still comes from sector–time variation in $\tau\rho_t$ interacted into $Z$, same source as the MLE. See `Lit-Papers/ELVIS.pdf` (primary source) and `Lit-Papers/schennach-2022-measurement-systems.pdf` (her 2022 *JEL* survey, §3.6.3) for the full derivation.

**Unified single-stage ELVIS — explored, held in reserve (2026-08-19).** The recast-in-$M$ move generalizes further — **there is only one true unobservable in the whole model: $M_{it}$**, for every firm-period, corp and unincorporated alike. Once $M_{it}$ is the ELVIS latent, the materials FOC and production identity solve in closed form with no $\mathcal V/\tilde{\mathcal W}$, no Jacobian, no density-transformation theorem needed at all:
$$\varepsilon_{it}(M_{it})=\ln\beta-\ln\!\left(\frac{\rho_tM_{it}}{P_tY_{it}}\right),\qquad \omega_{it}(M_{it})=(1-\beta)\ln M_{it}-\alpha_Kk_{it}-\alpha_Ll_{it}-\ln\beta+\ln\rho_t-\ln P_t$$
$y_{it}$ cancels out of $\omega_{it}(M_{it})$ — verified, not a bug: this is Gandhi–Navarro–Rivers's own $\Phi_{it}=y_{it}-\hat\varepsilon_{it}$ step, specialized to constant (Cobb-Douglas) elasticity. AR(1) moment reduces to $\eta_{it}(M_{it},M_{it-1})=\omega_{it}(M_{it})-\gamma_0-\gamma_1\omega_{it-1}(M_{it-1})$, no $\varepsilon$ left, needing only $E[\eta_{it}Z_{it}]=0$ (never the AR(1) innovation density). Corp/unincorp evasion relaxes from hard constraints to moments: corp's "$e\equiv0$" becomes a centering moment $E[e_{it}(M_{it})\mid\text{Corp}_i]=0$; unincorp's "evade on average" becomes an inequality moment via ELVIS's auxiliary-variable device. All parameters ($\beta,\alpha_K,\alpha_L,\gamma_0,\gamma_1,\lambda,\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2$) would be estimated jointly from the full panel, no separate first stage. Counterfactual revenue under this route: either a plain unweighted MC fallback, or an ELVIS-native auxiliary-parameter moment delivering a genuine interval for expected revenue. Set-identification mechanics: for candidate $\theta$, solve the inner problem to get $\hat L_n(\theta)$, evaluate over a grid, identified/confidence set is $\{\theta:\hat L_n(\theta)\le\text{critical value}\}$ from Chernozhukov–Hong–Tamer (2007) or Schennach's own conservative $\chi^2$ shortcut — not an ad hoc tolerance. **Status:** drafted as slides for `600-opt-tax.qmd`, kept in an HTML-comment reserve block, not merged into the live "recast-in-$M$" ELVIS section — sequencing was to show ELVIS fixing the MSM failure first (already live), only pitch the fully-unified version if that lands. Full derivation in `Paper/sections/9999-elvis.qmd`.

**Implementation details from the supplement/reference code (2026-08-25), evergreen.** Read `Lit-Papers/ELVIS_supplement.pdf` (32pp) and the reference GAUSS implementation in `Lit-Papers/ELVIS_code/` (`elvisutil.g`, `elvisexample.g`). (i) The confidence region should use the *profiled* statistic $\hat Q_n(\beta)=-(\sup_{\eta,\gamma}\hat L_n((\beta,\eta),\gamma)-\sup_{\theta,\gamma}\hat L_n(\theta,\gamma))$ — subtracting the global sup over the whole unrestricted model — not the raw $\hat L_n(\theta)$ against a threshold; (ii) Theorem F.1 hands a ready closed-form conservative critical value, $\{\theta:\sup_\gamma-2n\hat L_n(\theta,\gamma)\le\chi^2_{d_g,\alpha}\}$, no subsampling needed for a first pass — upgrade to Chernozhukov-Hong-Tamer (2007) subsampling only if a tighter region is wanted; (iii) two standard objective choices, both covered by her Definition F.1: empirical likelihood (Owen) or a CUE-type quadratic $-\tfrac12\hat g'\hat V^{-1}\hat g$ — the reference GAUSS code defaults to the CUE version; (iv) numerically, the reference code samples directly from the $\gamma$-tilted density via Metropolis (not a naive self-normalized importance-sampling ratio over the untilted $\rho$) — more robust with a bounded/awkward support like ours; (v) computationally, $\theta$ is gridded/profiled (its optimization problem is "difficult" — possibly a whole set of maximizers) while $(\text{nuisance},\gamma)$ is optimized via a derivative-free simplex (Nelder-Mead) for each $\theta$ (its problem is convex, unique global optimum).

**Sign/scaling convention for the eventual test statistic, evergreen (clarified against AK2020's actual code, 2026-08-31).** The $-2n\hat L_n(\theta,\gamma)\le\chi^2_{d_g,\alpha}$ formula is written in **Schennach's own sign convention**: her $\hat L_n$ is the *negative*, maximized CUE quadratic (her Definition F.1, $-\tfrac12\hat g'\hat V^{-1}\hat g$) — so $-2n\hat L_n=n\cdot g'V^{-1}g$, always non-negative, as a test statistic must be. Confirmed this is exactly what AK2020 do too, checked directly in their code (`/Volumes/SSD Hans/Github/TopicsDecisionMaking/ReplicationAK/FirstApp/`, also duplicated at `/Volumes/SSD Hans/Github/Adv Micro/Market Power/REP_0621/FirstApp/`): `cudafunctions/cuda_fastoptim.jl:67,100` defines their objective as `Qn2 = 1/2*dvecdum2'*Omega2*dvecdum2` — **positive**, **minimized** (opposite sign/direction from Schennach's $\hat L_n$) — and `procedures/1App_main.jl:78,96,103` builds their reported test statistic as `TSMC = 2*minf*n`, i.e. $2n\cdot Q_{n2,\min}$. Since $\hat L_n=-Q_{n2}$, this is algebraically identical to $-2n\hat L_n$ — the two conventions agree.
- **Why this matters for our own code, not just theory:** `cue_objective_common` in `Code/Deconvolution/1211-stage2-elvis-driver-AB.R` follows AK2020's convention exactly (`0.5*sum(d2^2/eig$values[pos])` — positive, minimized), not Schennach's. So `Lhat` (the `f$value` logged at every lambda point) is the AK2020-style object, not $\hat L_n$. **The correct test statistic is `TS = 2*n*Lhat`** (mirroring AK2020's literal `TSMC=2*minf*n`), compared against $\chi^2_{d_g,\alpha}$ — applying `-2n*Lhat` directly would be wrong, producing a negative statistic. $n$ is `n_used` (feasibility-filtered sample size); $d_g$ is the number of moment rows: checked directly in `Code/Rcpp/1200-stage2-elvis.cpp`, 8 for moment set A, `8+J+6` for moment set B ($J\approx29$ industries, so $d_g\approx43$ there — this was later corrected again to $8+J+7$ once the $h'$ score moment was added to B, see below).

## Stage-2 ELVIS coding sessions (2026-08-25 onward)

**Citation correction — the counterfactual's $E[U]$ worked example (2026-08-25).** `9999-elvis.qmd`'s "counterfactual without $f_\psi$" section previously mis-cited its worked example ("recovering $E[U]$") as being on p.1250 of @Schennach2014 — that paper is only pp. 345–385 (checked directly against `Lit-Papers/ELVIS.pdf`, page headers run 346→384). The actual source is her 2022 *Journal of Economic Literature* survey "Measurement Systems" (`Lit-Papers/schennach-2022-measurement-systems.pdf`, bib key `Schennach2021`), p. 1250, right after her Theorem 11 (≡ our Theorem 2.1): include $E[U-\tilde\theta]=0$ as an extra moment and read the possible values of $E[U]$ off the identified set for the auxiliary parameter $\tilde\theta$ — exactly the $\tilde\theta(\tilde\tau)$ device used for counterfactual revenue. Fixed in `9999-elvis.qmd`.

**Second real-world ELVIS implementation found — Aguiar & Kashaev 2020 (2026-08-25).** `Lit-Papers/AK2020.pdf` (*Review of Economic Studies*, "Stochastic Revealed Preferences with Measurement Error") builds an ELVIS-based test of revealed-preference rationality under measurement error, with a full working Julia/CUDA implementation at `TopicsDecisionMaking/ReplicationAK/` — a second concrete reference beyond Schennach's own GAUSS code, and the first one built for an applied economics moment system that mixes inequality and equality/centering moments like ours. Their Theorem 4 proves inequality moments $g_I$ never need their own $\gamma$-component (set-identified only via divergence to $+\infty$) and their code implements this literally: $g_I$ rows are dropped from the $\gamma$-searched moment vector entirely and enforced instead as a support restriction on the simulator. **Applied here:** the unincorp "evade positively on average" moment now has a documented alternative — drop it from $g$ and instead restrict the unincorp $M$-sampler to only propose $e(M^{(s)})>0$. Also confirmed by reading their code: objective is the same CUE quadratic, with $\hat\Omega^-$ built by eigendecomposing the tilted-moment covariance and dropping non-positive eigenvalues; solver is derivative-free bound-constrained BOBYQA (not Nelder-Mead), warm-started from a global pre-search and run twice as a cheap convergence check; the constraint-satisfying MCMC chain is generated **once** and reused across every $\gamma$-evaluation (and $\theta$-grid point) — chain generation, not the $\gamma$-search, is the expensive step; a convex per-unit QP supplies each unit's warm-start draw.

**Stage-2 ELVIS coding started (2026-08-25).** Implementation approach: code the **stage-2-only** ELVIS moment system first (θ=(λ,δ0,δ1,δ2), evasion-FOC moment recast in $M$), architected so the moment-row list and $M$-sampler extend cleanly to the full unified single-latent-$M$ model later; R driving Rcpp/C++ workers. **Caveat, corrected in-session:** `Code/Rcpp/100-mcmc.cpp` / `Code/Deconvolution/600-mcmc-h.R` (a Metropolis-tilted-moment sampler for the AR(1) drift) was an **exploratory branch never used in the actual paper** — the paper's own production-function/AR(1) identification runs through standard IV-GMM (`obj_fun_ivar1_bounds` + `ivreg::ivreg` in `021-deconv-funs.R`), not simulation. Treat that Rcpp file as a syntax reference only.
- **`stage2_data` built:** `Code/Deconvolution/1200-stage2-data.R` joins `df` (`Code/Products/931.1-fs-se-het.RData`) with industry-level $\alpha_K,\alpha_L,\beta$ from **both** instrument choices compared in the paper (`pf_list`: `lag_m`; `pf_list_l2w`: `lag_2_cal_W`), stacked with an `ins` tag. Output: `Code/Products/1200-stage2-data.RData` → `stage2_data`, 80,990 rows (40,495 per `ins` value; 8,117 corp / 32,378 unincorp firm-periods each).
- **$\tau\rho_t$ proxy, initial placeholder:** used $\tau_{\tilde C,it}=\tau_{S,it}-\tau_{P,it}\beta$ (`eff_tax_rate_c`), later superseded — see two-tax-rate entry below.
- **$\delta_0$ specification, deferred:** if $\tau\rho_t$ is ~constant within a sector-time cell, $\ln(\tau\rho_t)$ is collinear with $\delta_0$ under industry×year FE. Decision: start with $\delta_0$ as a plain scalar intercept; revisit splitting by sector-time only once $\lambda$'s identification is clear.

**Two-tax-rate ($\tau_S\ne\tau_P$) model, derived from scratch (2026-08-25).** Discovered while building `stage2_data`: `tau_rho_proxy`/`eff_tax_rate_c` is $\le0$ for 26.5% of unincorp firm-periods — a real refund regime, not noise, but $h(e)$ needs $\ln(\tau\rho_t)>0$. Re-deriving the firm's profit function with separate $\tau_S$ (sales-side) and $\tau_P$ (purchases-side) rates — full derivation in `Paper/sections/9999-tax-wedge.qmd` — gives two results: (i) the **evasion FOC only ever contains $\tau_P$**, never $\tau_S$ — `sales_tax_rate_purchases` is the theoretically correct $h(e)$ input, not the combined/effective proxy. Comparative static: $\partial e^*/\partial\tau_P>0$ (higher $\tau_P$ *increases* evasion — every unit of overreporting becomes more valuable whether caught or not), confirmed two ways (closed form under linear $q$, implicit function theorem via the SOC; an earlier claim of the opposite sign mixed up how the clawback term scales). **This cannot be checked by regressing $\mathcal V_{it}$ on $\tau_{P,it}$** — `sales_tax_rate_purchases=t2/s10` and `log_mats_share=log(s10/nom_gross_output)` both use `s10`=nominal $M^*_{it}$ in opposite positions, so $\mathcal V_{it}$ and $\tau_{P,it}$ are mechanically negatively linked regardless of the true economics. Verified empirically that this contamination is real: `feols(cal_V~sales_tax_rate_purchases|sic_3+year)` gives $-1.890^{***}$, and controlling for `sales_tax_rate_sales` jointly still gives $-2.025^{***}$ — the mechanical link survives conditioning, so neither regression is evidence against the (positive) theoretical prediction; (ii) the **materials FOC picks up a multiplicative $(1-\tau_S)/(1-\tau_P)$ wedge**, algebraically identical to redefining $\varepsilon_{it}(M_{it})$ via the ratio of net-of-tax nominal purchases to net-of-tax nominal sales rather than the gross ratio — both legs still directly-observed nominal quantities, so the "nominal ratio needs no price index" result survives unchanged. Downstream algebra ($\mathcal V=u-\varepsilon$, $\mathcal W=y-\beta(m-\mathcal V)$) is unaffected in form. **What needed changing:** (a) `first_stage_panel`/`first_stage_panel_me` in `021-deconv-funs.R` would need `var="log_mats_share_net"` instead of `"log_mats_share"` — this reopens **stage 1**, currently supervisor-approved/read-only, deliberately not applied; (b) `1200-stage2-data.R`/the driver need to switch the $h(e)$ input from `tau_rho_proxy` to `sales_tax_rate_purchases` directly — this one **was** applied, see below. This nests the currently-approved single-$\tau$ model exactly at $\tau_S=\tau_P$. Maintained-not-tested: $\tau_S,\tau_P$ are exogenous to the firm's $M,e$ choices.

**Follow-up sign checks, all inconclusive — deliberately not pursued further (2026-08-26).** Tried `log(t2_nom)` (nominal tax paid on purchases, a level not a rate) instead of the rate: comes back **positive** ($+0.175^{***}$ controlling for `log(t1_nom)`; $+0.0655^{***}$ univariate), matching the theoretical sign — but $t_2\equiv\tau_P\rho_tM^*_{it}$ by the model's own definition, so $\log(t_2)$ mechanically contains $\log M^*_{it}$ in the *same* direction as $\mathcal V_{it}$ this time (opposite-direction contamination from the rate version, not clean evidence either). Tried collapsing to sector-year cells (mean/median `sales_tax_rate_purchases` by `sic_3`×`year`) — **negative again** ($-0.961^{***}$ for the mean, $-0.253$ n.s. for the median). **Decision: stop chasing this sign with bivariate regressions** — a live untestable-from-here confound is that `sales_tax_rate_purchases` may partly reflect local enforcement intensity (stricter enforcement → rate closer to statutory *and* independently → less evasion via detection). The two legitimate ways to actually test $\partial e^*/\partial\tau_P$: (i) the 1983 fiscal-reform diff-in-diff (`300-fiscal-reform.qmd`, currently commented out of the build); (ii) the structural ELVIS/MSL estimation itself.

**Session paused (2026-08-26) — resume checklist for stage-2 ELVIS coding.** Working, tested code existed at this point: `1200-stage2-data.R`, `1205-stage2-warmstart.R` (NLS warm start), `Code/Rcpp/1200-stage2-elvis.cpp` (`mh_tilted_average_cpp`), `1210-stage2-elvis-driver.R` (CUE objective + BOBYQA per $\lambda$-grid-point). All four run against real data at least once.

**Item (b) applied — driver switched to $\tau_P$ directly, item (a) deliberately deferred (2026-08-27).** Switched `1205-stage2-warmstart.R` and `1210-stage2-elvis-driver.R`'s $h(e)$ input from `tau_rho_proxy`/`eff_tax_rate_c` to `sales_tax_rate_purchases` ($\tau_P$) directly — `Code/Rcpp/1200-stage2-elvis.cpp` needed no change (its `tau_rho` argument is agnostic to which R column feeds it). **First-stage estimates deliberately left as-is** — item (a) was explicitly not applied.
- **Positivity filter improved as a side effect:** $\tau_P\le0$ affects only ~10% of unincorp firm-periods (vs. ~26.5% for the old `eff_tax_rate_c`) — expected, since $\tau_P$ alone has no netting term that can flip its sign.
- **Warm start re-run (`1205`):** with $\tau_P$, drops 50.1% of unincorp obs for both `ins` choices (`e_hat<=0` now dominates, not $\tau_P\le0$) — down from ~58% under the old proxy. `lambda0` (99th-percentile-of-$\hat e$ starting value) optimized to the lower bound `1e-12` for both `ins` choices — a warm-start artifact, not diagnosed further at the time.
- **Driver smoke test (`ins="lag_m"`, 5-point $\lambda$ grid, `n_fast=500`):** $\hat L_n(\lambda)$ no longer monotonic across the grid (0.468 → 1.365 → 0.263 → 0.269 → 0.202) — smallest at the largest $\lambda$ tried, minimum possibly past the grid edge.

**Warm-start `M_hat` clipping fix, and what it revealed about $\hat\varepsilon$ heterogeneity (2026-08-27).** `1205-stage2-warmstart.R`'s plug-in $\hat M=M^*\exp(-(\mathcal V+\hat\varepsilon))$ (constant $\hat\varepsilon=$ median CORP epsilon) gives $\hat M>M^*$ (negative implied evasion) for **44.4%** of unincorp firm-periods — not measurement noise, but the model's own implication that a single constant plug-in must occasionally overshoot. Fixed by flooring: `M_hat = pmin(M_star*exp(-(cal_V+eps_hat)), M_star)`, `e_hat>=0` (an initial `min()`-instead-of-`pmin()` version was caught before running — `min()` on two vectors inside `mutate()` collapses to one global scalar). **Mechanism:** $\hat\varepsilon$ is a single fixed number, so the 44.4% figure reflects the share of unincorp firms with observed $\mathcal V_{it}<-\hat\varepsilon\approx0.070$ — a firm with little/no true evasion but a positive idiosyncratic shock ($\varepsilon_{it}>0$) shows low/negative $\mathcal V_{it}$ purely from that shock; the constant plug-in only adds back the population median $\varepsilon$. Confirmed empirically: median $\mathcal V_{it}$ (unincorp) $\approx0.12$, mean $\approx0.05$, left-skewed enough that 44.4% falls below the $0.070$ threshold.

**$\tau_P=0$ corner firms — full characterization (2026-08-27).** The ~10% of unincorp firm-periods with `sales_tax_rate_purchases<=0` are **all** `sales_tax_purchases (t2) == 0` exactly (verified at the Stata source, `col_df$t2`) — a clean three-way split, NA (26.2%) / exactly 0 (8.8%) / positive (65.0%), zero negative values, no imputation. Not a data problem: genuinely no tax paid on raw-material purchases that period.
- **Characteristics:** essentially no size difference from non-zero firms (capital, sales, materials all close); labor somewhat lower. **Heavily industry-concentrated**: sic 311 (food products, 40.4% zero share, ~15% of the whole unincorp sample) and 312/331/354/369 (26–33% zero share) vs. near-0% in apparel/footwear/electrical/metal-products. **86% ALSO report zero sales tax on sales** ($t_1=0$) — the signature of a genuinely VAT-exempt good (raw/basic foodstuffs) on both margins, not selective misreporting. Year pattern (highest 1981 at 21.7%, settling to 6–9% by the late 1980s) consistent with Colombia's VAT base broadening over the decade.
- **Economic logic:** cost is linear in $e$ ($MC$ constant in $e$), $MB(e)=\tau_P\rho_t(1-2\lambda e)$. At $\tau_P=0$, $MB(e)\equiv0$ for every $e$ while $MC>0$ always — $e^*=0$ strictly dominates for **any** $\theta$. $M$ is a known point ($M=M^*$), not latent, for these firms.
- **Key finding:** the literal FOC-corner inequality moment ($\psi_{it}\ge\ln(\tau_P\rho_t)-\delta_0+\delta_1\omega_{it}-\delta_2\omega_{it}^2$) has RHS $=-\infty$ at $\tau_P=0$ — vacuously satisfied for any $\theta$. These firms carry **zero identifying information about $(\lambda,\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2)$** through the evasion-FOC channel. Also rules out an $E[e]=U_{\text{corner}}$-style moment scoped to only these firms (would be exactly 0 by construction).
- **Order-condition check:** the smooth block $(\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2)$ (3 params) against $d_g=7$ moments in the then-current system was already overidentified by 4 restrictions without any corner-firm contribution.
- **Stage 1 left untouched deliberately again**, same boundary as item (a).
- **Implemented instead: a `corner_mode` toggle**, "drop" vs "include_zero", for later comparison: for `corner==1` firms $M$ is fixed at $M^*$ (a degenerate distribution's expectation is the point itself), psi-carrying moment rows set to exactly 0 (uninformative, not "satisfied at 0"), only `eps*om` computed. Smoke-tested both modes clean. `include_zero` (n: 20,471→22,462) showed a much lower $\hat L_n$ level with what looked like an actual interior minimum near $\lambda\approx4.87\text{e-}05$, vs. `drop`'s still-falling profile — a shape observation, not a result (levels aren't comparable across different-$n$ moment/weight matrices).

**BOBYQA convergence-tracking + gamma-bounds instability, and its fix: per-firm reseeded RNG + burn-in (2026-08-27).** Widening `1210`'s gamma box from $\pm20$ to $\pm100$ made $\hat L_n$ **worse at every single lambda point, in both corner modes** — impossible if BOBYQA were finding a real optimum. Capturing `res$convergence`/`res$iter` confirmed BOBYQA still reported "success" at these worse points. Root cause, found by checking both reference ELVIS implementations first: the Metropolis sampler was missing two things both `Lit-Papers/ELVIS_code/elvisutil.g` and `TopicsDecisionMaking/ReplicationAK/.../cuda_chainfun.jl` implement: (i) a deterministic **per-individual RNG reseed** before generating that firm's chain — the previous version drew accept/reject via `R::runif()`, which consumes R's global stream and so is NOT fixed across (theta,gamma) evaluations, injecting uncontrolled noise into the exact objective BOBYQA optimizes; (ii) an explicit **burn-in/equilibration phase** discarded before averaging — the previous version averaged from the very first Metropolis step, biasing the tilted average toward the untilted initial draw, worsening for larger $|\gamma|$ (slower to mix) — likely explaining why wider gamma bounds made things uniformly worse.
- **Fix:** `mh_tilted_average_cpp` now seeds a per-firm `std::mt19937_64` as `base_seed+row_id[i]` in C++ directly — the whole random sequence is identical for that firm at any (theta,gamma). New `n_burn`/`n_keep` arguments implement discard-then-average, `r` ranging `-n_burn+1 .. n_keep`.
- **Smoke-tested clean** (`n_burn=50,n_keep=500`) across all 4 combinations: `lag_m`/`drop`'s profile now smooth and mostly monotonic (0.476→0.11→0.109→0.021→0.0137). Convergence tracking earned its keep immediately: 2 of 20 lambda points hit `conv=5,iter=1000` (BOBYQA's `maxeval` cap, not real convergence).
- **Counterfactual note for later:** AK2020's cache-the-chain-and-reweight architecture was considered and rejected for the *estimation* loop (support depends on $\lambda$), but once $\hat\theta$ is fixed after estimation, the counterfactual's per-firm type draws only need to be drawn once — save realized draws directly rather than re-deriving from a seed.

**Stage-2 moment system, first finalized version — 15 moments (2026-08-28).** Replaced the old 7-moment system (`psi`, `psi·Z` for lagged K/L/cross-instrument, `eps·psi`, `om·psi`, `eps·om`) with 15, adding $\sigma^2_\psi$ as a fourth smooth parameter. $\gamma\in\mathbb R^{15}$, BOBYQA inner problem $4+15=19$-dimensional.
- **Dropped `psi*lag_k`/`psi*lag_l`/`psi*(cross-instrument)` entirely.** The closed-form materials-FOC inversion shows that given $\omega$, $M$ is a deterministic function of $(k,l)$ alone — so the lagged-K/L interactions were already, indirectly, testing $M\perp e\mid\omega$ rotated into observables. Replaced by `psi·ln(M)`, `psi·(ln M)^2` directly (used $\ln M$, not raw $M$, to avoid a raw-scale row dominating the covariance eigenvalues).
- **Rejected `E[psi*e]=0`** as tautological: given $\omega$, $e$ is the evasion FOC's own solution, a strictly monotonic invertible function of $\psi$ — not a testable independence.
- **Rejected `E[eM]-E[e]E[M]=0`** as actively misspecified (not merely uninformative) under $M\perp e\mid\omega$: by the law of total covariance, $\mathrm{Cov}(e,M)=E_\omega[\mathrm{Cov}(e,M\mid\omega)]+\mathrm{Cov}(E[e|\omega],E[M|\omega])$ — conditional independence kills only the first term; the second is generically nonzero since both $E[e|\omega]$ (U-shaped cost) and $E[M|\omega]$ (materials FOC) vary systematically with $\omega$.
- **New eps-heterogeneity block, matched per firm's own `sic_3`.** CORP $\varepsilon$'s mean is exactly zero within every industry (forced by stage-1's own per-industry OLS normal equations) but **variance is not homogeneous**: per-`sic_3` sd ranges 0.17–0.63 (pooled 0.42), Bartlett's $K^2=920$, $p<2.2\times10^{-16}$. New rows: $E[\varepsilon-\hat\mu_1]=0$, $E[(\varepsilon-\hat\mu_1)^2-\hat\sigma^2]=0$, $E[(\varepsilon-\hat\mu_1)^3-\hat\mu_3]=0$, $E[\psi\cdot(\varepsilon-\hat\mu_1)^2]=0$ — targets computed per `(sic_3,ins)` in `Code/Deconvolution/1206-stage2-eps-targets.R`.
- **New $\sigma^2_\psi$ nuisance parameter**, own moment $E[\psi^2-\sigma^2_\psi]=0$, unlocking a heteroskedasticity-in-$\psi$ block against $\omega,\omega^2,\varepsilon_c$. Same "anchor-then-multiply" logic throughout: any quantity pinned to exact zero mean by its own moment can be safely multiplied by anything else for a clean $\mathrm{Cov}=0$ test.
- **Dropped `psi·(eps^2-var)` as redundant** with `psi·eps_c^2` given `psi`'s own row already enforces $E[\psi]=0$.
- **Implemented and smoke-tested; not yet run at real scale** at the time of this entry.

**Warm-start redesign (2026-08-28) — S-draw $\varepsilon$ sampling per firm, replacing the single global constant.** Fix for the 44.4%-clipped-to-zero finding above: for each unincorp firm, draw $S=200$ values of $\varepsilon$ by bootstrap-resampling from that firm's own industry's CORP $\varepsilon$ pool, stack into a long dataset, fit the U-shape NLS on the stacked sample. This is exactly the naive-averaging construction rejected for the *final* estimator (MSM rejection above) — used here only as a BOBYQA starting point, which doesn't need to be consistent, only close.
- **$\sigma^2_\psi$ falls out for free** as $\overline{\hat\psi^2}$ on the fitted residuals — a zero-cost byproduct, not separately optimized.
- **Latent bug fixed in passing:** `quantile()`'s return carries a name (e.g. `"99%"`), silently producing `"lambda.99%"` instead of `"lambda"` when combined via `c(lambda=quantile(...), ...)` — existed pre-redesign too, only surfaced once name-based lookup (`fit$par[["lambda"]]`) was introduced. Fixed with `unname()`.
- **Smoke-tested at real scale:** $S=200$, ~5.8M stacked rows each `ins`, ~64s. `sigma2_psi_init` came back plausible (1.46 for `lag_m`, 1.84 for `lag_2_cal_W`). $\hat L_n$ at the smoke-test's single $\lambda$ point dropped from 0.78 (old placeholder start) to 0.21 (new warm start).
- **Not fixed, deliberately out of scope:** $\lambda_0$ still optimizes to `1e-12` for both `ins` choices — evidently not specific to the eps-plug-in method, likely intrinsic to the crude NLS loss surface. Doesn't block `1210` since $\lambda$ is gridded there, not seeded from the warm start.

**Two follow-up fixes to the warm start, same day (2026-08-28).**
- **Common-random-numbers gap, fixed.** A single `set.seed(opt$seed)` at file-top meant the two `get_warmstart()` calls for the two `ins` choices consumed one shared RNG stream sequentially — not reproducible in isolation. Fixed: `set.seed(opt$seed + INS_SEED_OFFSET[[ins_choice]])` called inside `get_warmstart()` itself. Verified by swapping call order and confirming byte-identical output.
- **Moment coverage made explicit, extended to 4.** The prior plain-SSR objective (`sum(psi_hat^2)`) silently satisfied 3 of the real system's moments (`psi=0`, `om*psi=0`, `psi*om^2=0`) as an unstated OLS byproduct, but `psi*lnM=0` wasn't covered at all. Made explicit: 4-moment, 4-parameter exactly-identified GMM (`g(theta) = (E[psi], E[om*psi], E[psi*om^2], E[psi*ln M])`), equal-weighted, `sum(g(theta)^2)`. $\lambda_0$ still hit `1e-12` under yet another objective — reinforcing it's intrinsic to the crude warm-start problem, not any particular eps-sampling or moment choice.

**Parallelization over firms, two iterations (2026-08-28).** Each firm's Metropolis chain was already fully independent — pure engineering, not a design question.
- **First: `mclapply`.** `cue_objective` gained an `n_cores` argument; `dat` split via `parallel::splitIndices`, each chunk run through `mh_tilted_average_cpp` in a separate forked process, results `rbind`ed back. Verified bit-identical to serial at both toy and realistic scale. Speedup at realistic scale (`n≈2900`, `n_burn=100,n_keep=1000`): ~4.7x at 8 cores, ~5.1x at 11 (12-core machine) — sub-linear (fork + serialization overhead) but real; toy scale only showed ~1.6x — don't judge parallelization gains from toy smoke tests. `run-stage2-elvis-sweep.sh` gained a 4th positional arg (`N_CORES`) so the sweep's own 4-way process parallelism and `mclapply`'s thread count could be sized together without oversubscribing.
- **Superseded same day: replaced with RcppParallel after actually measuring the overhead the earlier conclusion claimed didn't matter.** The "no need for RcppParallel" conclusion was premature — based on "beats serial," not "captures the available gain." Measured directly: pure fork+collect overhead of one `mclapply(mc.cores=8)` call, isolated in the actual loaded session, is ~8.65ms, paid on every single BOBYQA objective evaluation (`mclapply` re-forks from scratch each call). At realistic scale, `mclapply` was capturing ~4.9x of a theoretical ~8x ceiling — real headroom left on the table.
- **Implemented:** the per-firm loop restructured into an `RcppParallel::Worker` (`TiltedMomentWorker`), dispatched via `RcppParallel::parallelFor` — a TBB-backed thread pool created once and reused across every call from the same R session. Low-risk refactor: per-firm computation was already thread-safe by construction, so the only real correctness fix needed was switching every per-firm scratch buffer from `Rcpp::NumericVector` (touches R's allocator, unsafe off the main thread) to `std::array<double,15>` (stack-allocated), with R-facing I/O wrapped in RcppParallel's `RVector`/`RMatrix`.
- **Measured results:** bit-identical across 1/8/12 threads. Speedup: 6.14x at 8 threads, 8.60x at 12 — both better than mclapply's 4.9x, and 12-thread exceeded the naive 8x ceiling estimate because of a second win: the `std::array` switch removed R-API overhead from the single-threaded baseline too (86.0ms vs. old 174.5ms) — a leaner per-firm computation on top of which threading stacks. Combined: original serial-with-NumericVector (174.5ms) to new 12-thread RcppParallel (10.0ms) is a **~17.5x** total improvement.
- **R-side simplified:** `cue_objective` dropped the `mclapply`/`splitIndices` branching entirely, back to a single direct call. Thread count set once via `RcppParallel::setThreadOptions` right after CLI args parsed; `n_cores` removed from `fit_one_lambda`/`run_stage2_elvis`'s signatures. `library(parallel)` → `library(RcppParallel)`, added via `renv::install()`.

**First real smoke test under the 15-moment system + new warm start (2026-08-29) — economically sensible, but with a genuine open problem.** All 4 configs (`ins`×`corner_mode`, smoke defaults, `GAMMA_BOUND=100`), all 20 fits converged. Good news: $\delta_2>0$ in every cell (0.30–0.59) — the U-shape opens the right way — and $\omega^*=\delta_1/(2\delta_2)$ clusters tightly at 2.0–2.7, landing almost exactly in the CORP-firm empirical $\omega$ distribution's 1st-quartile-to-median range (2.03–2.91).
- **Bad news: $\gamma$ pins near the box bound in many cells** (up to 99.9 against `GAMMA_BOUND=100`). Re-ran with `GAMMA_BOUND=300`: gammas **climbed with the bound** (many now 265–300) rather than settling — real instability. Breaking down which moment carries the largest $|\gamma|$ per cell across all 20: `eps*om` most often (7/20), then `psi*eps_c^2` and `psi*om^2` (4/20 each), `om*psi` (3/20) — a cluster of $\omega$-involving rows.
- **First hypothesis (within-firm $\omega$ variance shrinks with $\lambda$) — real but incomplete.** Within-firm range of $\omega(M)$ shrinks from 26% of the across-firm spread at small $\lambda$ to 5.7% at large — confirmed, real, but doesn't fully explain the pattern since $\max|\gamma|$ is already elevated at the *smallest* $\lambda$ too.
- **Second hypothesis (mechanical bias in `eps*om` from $\omega=\tilde{\mathcal W}-(1-\beta)\varepsilon$) — derived, then refuted by the algebra itself.** Substituting through, the $(1-\beta)\mathrm{Var}(\varepsilon)$ terms **cancel exactly**, leaving $E[\varepsilon\omega]=\mathrm{Cov}(\varepsilon,\omega)$ with no mechanical bias. Checked on CORP data anyway: $\mathrm{Cov}(\varepsilon,\omega)\approx-0.148$ pooled, ranging $-0.004$ to $-0.42$ by industry, correlating 0.897 with $\mathrm{Var}(\varepsilon|j)$ across industries — looked like a real violation of $\varepsilon\perp\omega$.
- **That CORP check was then itself invalidated.** $\varepsilon$'s distribution is assumed firm-type-invariant (checkable on CORP), but $\omega$'s AR(1) innovation $\eta$ is **not** assumed independent of corp/non-corp status — CORP's $\omega$ distribution doesn't generalize to the unincorp estimation sample, so the $-0.148$ figure isn't informative about anything the estimator actually uses.
- **Built a proper diagnostic instead:** `tilted_omega_diag_cpp` — runs the exact same per-firm Metropolis chain as the estimator but returns per-firm tilted-average $\omega,\omega^2,e,M$. Ran on `lag_m-drop`'s $\lambda$-grid-point-3 fitted $(\theta,\gamma)$: Bartlett $K^2=3503.7$ for $\hat\omega$ across industries — bigger than $\varepsilon$'s own $K^2=920$ — and unlike $\varepsilon$, $\omega$ varies hugely in **both mean and variance** by industry (sic 352 at $\bar\omega\approx-3.7$ vs. others at $+1.9$ to $+4.2$; spot-checked, not a bug — a genuine industry-specific level in the AR(1)-regression residual).
- **Diagnosis and fix agreed:** pooling raw $\omega$ across industries with this much heterogeneity exposes the same law-of-total-covariance contamination flagged twice before — fix is jointly-estimated per-industry nuisance parameters $\mu_{\omega,j}$ (can't use a CORP-style external target, per the corp/non-corp point) and $\sigma_{\omega,j}$, each with its own defining moment.
- **General "anchor" principle established, from Schennach's own Example 1.5 (Continued), p.372:** her $X^*$ (not mean-zero) appears raw at any power, multiplied against her mean-zero residuals — she never demeans $X^*$ itself. Generalizes: an anchor (zero-mean by its own defining moment) from one "side" can multiply a raw quantity from another side, at any order, and stays valid regardless of the raw quantity's own heterogeneity.
- **Enumerating by order surfaced a combinatorial explosion, not resolved this session.** Systematically walking order 2/3/4 cross-moments plus the two new 29-row defining-moment blocks projected to $d_g\approx77$, $\theta_{\text{smooth}}$ from 4 to 62, BOBYQA inner dimension to roughly 139. Session paused explicitly to reconsider size, not to finalize this list — see the gamma-counting correction below, which resolved this differently.

**Gamma-counting corrected + moment sets A/B replace the combinatorial-explosion plan (2026-08-29, same day).** Two fixes, checked against primary sources before implementing:
- **Gamma is unbounded, per Schennach herself.** ELVIS.pdf p.359: "solving for $\gamma$ includes considering solutions at infinity... the conditional distribution of the unobservable is typically degenerate" — a boundary solution is expected, not a numerical failure. AK2020 uses $\pm\infty$ bounds throughout, including on a warm-started refinement pass (bounds not narrowed). Confirmed `nloptr::bobyqa` handles `lower=-Inf`/`upper=Inf` correctly. `GAMMA_BOUND` removed; a `max|gamma|` column printed per lambda point to watch it.
- **Gamma's dimension does NOT count against the order condition.** Checked ELVIS.pdf p.349 (original Example 1.5: 5 moments, $\theta$ 1-dimensional) against p.372 (27 moments, $\theta$ 4-dimensional **including** 3 nuisance — never "4+27"), then confirmed mechanically in `Lit-Papers/ELVIS_code/`: `gam` is sized automatically to the moment count and optimized *jointly with the nuisance parameters* — `theta` proper never includes it. This retroactively explains why the "$d_g\approx77$, BOBYQA dim $\approx139$" estimate had been overcounting the identification burden — the fix needed was a smaller moment LIST, not a reinterpretation of the big one.
- **Two new moment systems built from scratch** (old 15-moment system + its `1210` driver left untouched as a reference point): new `mh_tilted_average_A_cpp`/`_B_cpp` in `Code/Rcpp/1200-stage2-elvis.cpp`, new driver `Code/Deconvolution/1211-stage2-elvis-driver-AB.R`.
  - **(A) 8 moments**, $\theta_{\text{smooth}}=(\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2)$: `psi`, `eps`, `psi·lnM`, `psi·om`, `psi·om²`, `eps·lnM`, `eps·e`, `eps·om` — all raw, no per-industry demeaning anywhere. `eps` (not `eps_c`) used raw and pooled since its industry-conditional mean is already ~0 by stage-1's own OLS normal equations.
  - **(B) = A's 8 + 7 more.** One genuinely new jointly-estimated block ($\mu_{m,j}$, industry mean of $\ln M$ — must be jointly estimated since $\ln M$ is the ELVIS latent itself, no external proxy) plus two externally, cheaply computed per-industry omega targets ($\hat\mu_{\omega,j}$, $\hat\sigma_{\omega,j}$, from $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ which is directly observed for unincorp too — built once in `Code/Deconvolution/1207-stage2-omega-targets.R`, sanity-checked $\sigma_{\omega,j}>0$ in 57/58 cells). $\mu_{m,j}$ needs $J$ separate industry-indicator rows (confirmed with the user a single pooled row would underidentify $J$ separate values) — exactly identified for that sub-block. Cross-terms stay pooled single rows, verified valid via $E[\psi\cdot(X-\mu_{X,j(i)})]=E_j[\mathrm{Cov}(\psi,X\mid j)]$ exactly (the anchor's own construction kills the between-industry contamination term that broke `E[eM]-E[e]E[M]`).
  - **All 4 combinations smoke-tested clean.** One real bug found+fixed: $\mu_{m,j}$'s per-lambda box bounds originally used a `DELTA_BOUND=20` fallback for an industry absent at a given lambda — wrong scale for $\ln(M^*)$ (routinely $>e^{20}$ in nominal COP), causing `x0 > ub` errors under `include_zero`. Fixed by computing $\mu_{m,j}$'s init/bounds once from the full run sample.
  - **Two-pass BOBYQA refinement implemented** (AK2020's own practice): run once, re-run warm-started from that result with the same unbounded bounds as a cheap convergence check.

**2026-08-30: several real bugs found+fixed while running A/B at scale, plus a much better grid-construction method.**
- **`draw_from_rho` M>=0 bug fixed.** The support $(M^*-\frac1{2\lambda},M^*)$ can go negative when $M^*<\frac1{2\lambda}$. Previously such firms were DROPPED per lambda instead of having support truncated at 0 — caused sample size (and $\hat L_n$'s whole shape) to swing wildly and non-monotonically across the grid, which originally made `drop` vs `include_zero` look like it mattered more than it really did. Fixed: floors at $\max(0,\ldots)$; no firm ever dropped for feasibility (`n_used` constant across the grid).
- **Corner-firm handling settled: `include_zero` only, `"drop"` retired.** Compared at matched scale after the M>=0 fix: both agree on the same best lambda, `include_zero` adds real info without distortion.
- **Relative-evasion floor $\eta$ added.** Nothing in the moment system penalizes $M\to0$ (CUE's eigenvalue-truncated weighting down-weights high-variance directions rather than penalizing them — a real risk, not just theoretical). Fixed via a sampler restriction (AK2020 Theorem 4 style): `draw_from_rho_eta` floors $M\ge\eta M^*$, a new box-bounded $\eta\in[0,1)$ jointly optimized with $\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2$. Chose relative over absolute since $M^*$ spans orders of magnitude. In every run so far $\hat\eta\approx0$ — the data doesn't want this floor to bind, but it's a structural guarantee now.
- **$\mu_{m,j}$ upper-bound bug fixed.** Since $M\le M^*$ always, $E[\ln M\mid j]\le E[\ln M^*\mid j]$ — the original bound had this backwards. Fixed to `mean(log M*|j)-0.01`.
- **`DELTA_BOUND` widened 20→60** after B's quantile scan showed $\delta_0$ pinned exactly at $-20$ for `lag_m` at every $\lambda$ past the smallest — confined to the large-$\lambda$ uninteresting plateau, but worth not being silently box-constrained regardless.
- **Grid construction: quantile-of-$M^*$ grids are the wrong tool for reading $\hat L_n(\lambda)$'s shape.** (i) quantiles compress unevenly (prob 0.9999→0.9 spans $\lambda$ over 300x); (ii) more importantly, under linear $q$, $q$ at a firm's own $M^*$ saturates at 0.5 once $M^*$ exceeds the mean audit threshold $\frac1{2\lambda}$ — any $\lambda$ large enough that the median firm's $q\ge0.5$ is uninformative. Right primitive: $q@\text{median}=\lambda\cdot\text{median}(M^*)$, gridded from ~0.1% up to 50% directly — not $M^*$-quantile probabilities.
- **Two-stage grid protocol:** (1) quantile/hand-picked scan for the region of interest, (2) `bracket_local_min()`/`build_linear_refinement()` — finds the argmin, brackets it (extending past a grid edge by the SAME MULTIPLICATIVE ratio as the adjacent gap, not additive, since these grids are ratio-spaced), builds an equally-spaced grid over the bracket. Applied twice this session, narrowing from $[3.8\text{e-}8,1.15\text{e-}6]$ to $[9.1\text{e-}9,1.6\text{e-}7]$.
- **A `lag_2_cal_W`-specific instability found:** `max|gamma|` climbs steadily as $\lambda$ increases (0.92→...→408.2) for `lag_2_cal_W`, `lag_m` stays clean (<2) across the identical grid. At the shared minimum both look equally clean — flagged as a possible future signal that `lag_2_cal_W` is the weaker instrument, decision deliberately deferred.
- **Critical-value scaling derived and cross-checked** against both Schennach's Theorem F.1 and AK2020's Theorem 5 (they agree once translated into our own `cue_objective_common`'s units): $TS_n(\lambda)=2n\times\text{Lhat\_ours}(\lambda)$ vs. $\chi^2_{d_g,\alpha}$; for a $\lambda$-only confidence set (nuisance profiled out), $\hat Q_n(\lambda)=\text{Lhat\_ours}(\lambda)-\min_{\lambda'}\text{Lhat\_ours}(\lambda')$.
- **Economic read of the linear-$q$ estimates (provisional at the time):** at the small-$\lambda$ region A favored, detection probability under 1-3% for the median firm even at total hypothetical evasion, saturating near-certain only for the top 1-5% by size — flagged risk this could be the model sliding toward the $\lambda\to0$ limit rather than a real finding (this risk was later confirmed real; see the trim-cutoff sections below).
- **B's performance bottleneck diagnosed by direct measurement:** user hypothesized R-side matrix inversion was slow. Measured: one `mh_tilted_average_B_cpp` call (32,378 firms) ~0.52s; R-side linear algebra ~0.022s — simulation 24x more expensive, moving inversion to C++/BLAS would save at most ~4%. Real cost driver: B's 76-dimensional BOBYQA inner problem needing thousands of evaluations at ~0.5s each.

**Planned optimization, scoped 2026-08-31: move `cue_objective_common`'s linear algebra into C++/Accelerate — plus a free algebraic simplification found while scoping it.** User wanted to eventually use Apple's Accelerate (vecLib/LAPACK) instead of R's `solve()`.
- **Found first: `solve(V2)` in `cue_objective_common` is mathematically redundant, verified numerically to ~1e-15.** `A`'s columns are `Omega`'s own eigenvectors, so $V_2=A'\Omega A$ is exactly diagonal by orthonormality — `solve(V2)` is just `diag(1/eig$values[pos])`. The whole function collapses to `d2 <- t(A) %*% dvec; 0.5 * sum(d2^2 / eig$values[pos])`, dropping two matrix multiplies and the `solve()` call entirely. Flagged to the user before applying (numerical-core function).
- **Honest scope check:** matrices here are tiny ($d_g\le21$); the per-firm Metropolis sampler (already C++/RcppParallel, thousands of firms × `n_burn+n_keep` steps) is almost certainly the dominant cost by orders of magnitude. The plausible win from C++ is removed R-interpreter/dispatch overhead per BOBYQA call, not raw compute — worth profiling (`Rprof()`) before investing effort.
- **Implementation route preference:** RcppArmadillo (`arma::eig_sym`, `arma::solve`) over raw Accelerate LAPACK calls — far less code, and Armadillo links against Accelerate as its BLAS/LAPACK backend when configured, avoiding the raw Fortran-interface gotchas below.
- **Raw-Accelerate gotcha, if ever pursued:** as of macOS 13.3+/Xcode 15, Accelerate's LAPACK symbols are exported under Apple-specific names (`$NEWLAPACK` suffix), not standard Fortran names. Also: `dgesv` (general LU solve, the routine on Apple's docs link) is the wrong routine here — `Omega`/`V2` are symmetric PSD, so `dsyev`/`dsyevr` or Cholesky-based `dpotrf`+`dpotri` would be the matching routines.
- **Checked, not hypothetical:** this R install (4.6) does NOT use Accelerate — links its own bundled reference BLAS/LAPACK (`libRblas.dylib`, `libRlapack.dylib`). A genuine Accelerate-backed route would be a real, not-already-captured speedup for whatever linear algebra it touches, making RcppArmadillo doubly attractive (upgrades the backend for the project's C++ code without a system-wide R rebuild).

**Next methodological improvement flagged 2026-08-31, then corrected same day: calibrate the $\lambda$ grid off $\mathcal V$'s own distribution instead of $M^*$ — the first version of this idea was wrong.** First pass: `build_lambda_grid`'s $M^*$-quantile proxy is conservative (since $e\le M^*$ pointwise) but loose; proposed reusing `1212-stage2-A-prelim-plot.R`'s $E[\mathcal V]=E[u]-E[\varepsilon]$ check as the basis for grid construction instead. **Caught by the user the same day: this is itself wrong.** $u=\ln((M+e)/M)$ is **nonlinear** in $e$, so $\mathcal V=u-\varepsilon$ never decomposes additively into $e$ regardless of which operator ($E[\cdot]$ or $\text{Med}(\cdot)$) is applied — there's no shortcut through $\mathcal V$'s own moments to $e$.
- **Correct fix, implemented and run:** treat $E[e]$ and $\text{Med}[e]$ as genuine ELVIS **auxiliary parameters** (Schennach's own $E[U]$ device) — $E[e(M)]-\mu_e=0$ (identifies the mean) vs. $E[\mathbb 1(e(M)\le\theta_e)-\tfrac12]=0$ (identifies the median; a literal "$\text{Med}[e]-\theta_e=0$" is not itself a valid moment). Evaluated post-hoc at the already-fitted $(\hat\theta,\hat\gamma)$ via new export `tilted_e_diag_A_cpp`, reusing the exact per-firm chain (independence-sampler MH means `M_current` was already dead code after the first draw — only `g_current`/`e_current` carried forward on acceptance). Driven by `Code/Deconvolution/1213-stage2-A-aux-e.R`.
- **Results at the already-fitted best $\hat\lambda$ per `ins`:** `lag_m` — $\hat\mu_e=5{,}269$, $\hat\theta_e=569$, implied detection probabilities 0.62%/0.067%; `lag_2_cal_W` — $\hat\mu_e=3{,}858$, $\hat\theta_e=184$, 0.23%/0.011%. $\hat\mu_e\gg\hat\theta_e$ in both — right-skewed evasion (corner firms at $e\equiv0$ plus a long right tail among interior firms), a plausible feature not noise.
- **Implication for grid-building:** `tilted_e_diag_A_cpp` needs an already-fitted $(\hat\theta,\hat\gamma)$ — circular for building the *initial* grid. The right non-circular V-informed proxy is the warm-start's own construction (`1205`'s `e_hat = M_star - pmin(M_star*exp(-(cal_V+eps_s)), M_star)`), which needs no fitted $\theta,\gamma$ — flagged for a follow-up, not applied to any code.

**Moment set B converges for real, overnight run (2026-09-01).** B's own smoke test (2026-08-31) hit `conv=5` (BOBYQA's `maxeval` cap) at every single lambda point — B's inner problem is ~75-dimensional vs. A's 12. Fixed, scoped to B only: `xtol_rel` loosened to `1e-4` (real MC noise from the finite chain — resolving to 1e-6 is tighter than the noise floor supports), `maxeval` raised to 5000, `maxtime=600s` added. Launched overnight (`n_burn=500,n_keep=1000`, both `ins`, `caffeinate -dims`) — 31 of 32 lambda points converged for real; only one non-final quantile-phase point hit the `maxtime` cap. Runtime: `lag_m` ~3.95h, `lag_2_cal_W` ~4.23h.
- **Results:** `lag_m` — $\hat\lambda=5.88\times10^{-7}$, $\hat\delta_2=0.183$, $\hat\omega^*=3.38$ (close to A's 3.34). `lag_2_cal_W` — $\hat\lambda=5.88\times10^{-7}$, $\hat\delta_2=0.064$, $\hat\omega^*=6.97$ (A gave 2.35 — a large jump, flagged as needing understanding).
- **Correction, same day: not actually "all four fits share the edge issue" — caught by the user.** Three of the four (A's `lag_2_cal_W`, B's `lag_m`, B's `lag_2_cal_W`) genuinely share the issue: minimum at $\lambda=5.877\times10^{-7}$, refinement grid extended past the quantile grid's own edge and still falling as it approaches. **A's `lag_m` is different:** its quantile-phase minimum landed on an *interior* point, so its refinement window never tested anything smaller than the quantile grid already had — its own minimum sits at that window's edge, but nothing below it has ever been tried. That's an untested gap, not the same evidence.

**Same day, follow-up (2026-09-01): the $\hat\omega^*$-percentile diagnostic sharpens the `lag_2_cal_W` concern into a genuine economic-plausibility problem, points at `lag_m` as more trustworthy.** New `tilted_e_omega_diag_A_cpp`/`_B_cpp` (post-hoc-at-fitted-$(\hat\theta,\hat\gamma)$, same pattern as the $E[e]$ check): where does $\hat\omega^*$ sit in the actual tilted $\omega$ distribution?
- `lag_m`: 71.9th percentile (A), 71.0th (B) — stable across both moment sets, moderate and plausible.
- `lag_2_cal_W`: 29.9th percentile (A) vs. 99.9th (B) — the same instrument swinging between two awkward extremes depending on moment set.
- **Conclusion:** `lag_m`'s cross-moment-set *stability*, not just its point estimate, is evidence it's the more trustworthy instrument. `lag_2_cal_W` needs to be understood before trusting either of its numbers.
- Also added: $e$'s detection probability at P90/P95/P99 (stays under 50% ceiling in all four fits); explained why $E[e]$'s implied detection probability exceeds even P80 in three of four fits — $e$'s pooled distribution is extremely heavy-tailed (top 1% hold 32-35% of pooled $e$ mass, top 5% hold 62-65%), pulling the mean above the 80th percentile.
- **Instrument notation clarified:** `lag_m` is $m^*_{it-1}$, `lag_2_cal_W` is $\mathcal{W}_{it-2}$ — corrected throughout slides.
- **New exploratory artifact:** `1215-stage2-profile-CI-explore.R` — first look at Schennach's Theorem F.1 profile-CI shortcut applied to the (at-the-time) current grids. In all four combinations, only the argmin (plus one adjacent point for one config) fell inside the 95% band — the true lower bound wasn't resolved by that grid, consistent with the edge-of-grid issue.

**Meeting with Nail Kashaev (AK2020's own co-author) corrects the inference plan on four points, all verified against primary sources (2026-09-04).**
- **(1) Auxiliary-parameter moments must be jointly optimized, never read off a post-hoc tilted sample.** Schennach (2022 JEL), p.1250, right after Theorem 11: *"One should be careful not to interpret the entropy maximizing distribution as the actual distribution of the unobservables... one could merely include $E[U-\tilde\theta]=0$ among the moment conditions and the identified set for the auxiliary parameter $\tilde\theta$ will indicate the possible values of $E[U]$."* This is a real correction to work already in the codebase: `tilted_e_diag_A_cpp`, `tilted_omega_diag_cpp`, `tilted_e_omega_diag_A_cpp` fix $(\hat\theta,\hat\gamma)$ from the main fit, then run the tilted sampler once and report its plain average/pooled quantile as "the" mean/median — exactly the interpretation error the passage warns against. **Not yet fixed at the time of this entry**: correct construction adds $\tilde\theta$ as its own parameter with its own moment, solved jointly, reading the *identified set* off the resulting objective. All the 2026-08-31/09-01 $E[e]$/$\text{Med}[e]$/$\omega$-percentile diagnostic numbers should be treated as informal/exploratory, not estimates, until redone this way.
- **(2) When more than one parameter is "of interest," grid them jointly (a surface), profile only the true nuisance parameters.** ELVIS supplement, Example 1.5 Continued (pp.13–14): with $\theta=(\theta_1,\theta_2)$ both of interest, produces one joint critical-region surface (Fig. S.3), not two separate profiled curves.
- **(3) The counterfactual CI is a test inversion, following AK2020 §5.5 and their Appendix F code directly** (`Lit-Papers/ReplicationAK2020/Appendix_F/`). Confirmed by reading `F_main_shell.jl`/`F_figure1.R`: they grid the counterfactual price shifter `kap` (analog of $\Delta$) and the counterfactual object of interest `bshare` (analog of expected revenue $R$) jointly. At each cell, nuisance resolved once (convex-QP warm start), $\gamma$ ($d_g=5$) optimized via **three** successive unbounded BOBYQA passes (corrects an earlier "two-pass" note), each warm-started from the last. `TSMC=2*minf*n` logged per cell, matching our sign convention exactly. `F_figure1.R` inverts the test literally: `crit.value=qchisq(.95,df=5)`; for each `kap`, the CI on `bshare` is `[min,max]` over grid points with `TSMC<crit.value`.
- **(4) Nail's suggestion to port the codebase to C/Fortran, noted but not adopted as-is.** The RcppParallel/Accelerate work already measured ~17.5x on the actual bottleneck; the CUE linear algebra it was compared against is microseconds by comparison. A full port would be a much bigger lift than what's left on the table — worth profiling before committing.

**The global $\lambda$ lower-bound idea (from $M^*$) is dropped — the fix predates this write-up (2026-09-04).** The per-$\lambda$ feasibility filter (`M_star>1/(2*lambda)`, dropping infeasible firms) no longer exists in `1211`/`1200-stage2-elvis-common.h` — see the 2026-08-30 `draw_from_rho` fix above (support truncated directly at the physical floor, $\max(0,M^*-\frac1{2\lambda})$, instead of dropping firms). The relative-evasion cap $\eta$ (2026-08-30 entry) is the surviving, jointly-optimized second floor. Checked industry-level $M^*$ heterogeneity along the way ($1/(2\min_j M^*_j)$ ranges 0.0035–0.031 across top-10 industries, ~9x spread) but this is moot now that no global $\lambda$ floor is needed — kept only as context in case a future industry-specific $\lambda$ is pursued.

**`1205-stage2-warmstart.R`'s own `lambda_min <- 1/(2*max(e_hat))` is not a bug but isn't doing real feasibility work either (2026-09-04).** Unlike the real estimator, `e_hat` here is a fixed pre-computed plug-in, not a function of $\lambda$ — no analogous "$M_{\text{true}}>0$ fails at small $\lambda$" risk to protect against. Checked numerically: `max(e_hat)`$\approx2.67\times10^7$ (one extreme outlier draw) gives `lambda_min`$\approx1.87\times10^{-8}$ — ~5 orders of magnitude below `lambda_max`, essentially non-binding, close to the old `1e-12` placeholder. Real run (`S=250`) converged cleanly, `lambda_f`$\approx0.000131$ for both `ins`, landing at `lambda0`'s own P75-based starting value — not pinned at the floor. If a more meaningful floor is ever wanted, a high percentile of `e_hat` (P99.9 $\approx9.8\times10^{-7}$) would track "where the ceiling starts to matter for a non-extreme firm" much better than the sample max.

**SSH access to a second machine set up for additional compute (2026-09-05).** MacBook Pro at `hans@192.168.0.28`, passwordless key-based SSH from the Mac mini (key generated without a passphrase, a deliberate LAN-automation convenience/security tradeoff, flagged to the user). 12 cores, Intel x86_64 (~4x slower than the Mac mini for this workload, not chased further). R 4.6.1 + `tidyverse`/`Rcpp`/`RcppParallel`/`nloptr` installed, `Code/` synced via `rsync`, compiles clean. See `reference_macbook_ssh` memory for operational gotchas (PATH, `screen` for persistence, a recurring rsync-direction data-corruption bug) before using it again.

**Trim-cutoff decision: 1% (top 1% of interior firms by $M^*$ dropped), full investigation (2026-09-05).**
- **First attempt at this sweep (14 independent trim levels) was unusably noisy** — CI bounds jumped by 2-4 orders of magnitude between adjacent trim levels differing by a handful of firms. Root cause: every trim level's BOBYQA chain reset to the same untrimmed-sample warm start, a poor starting point for heavily-trimmed samples.
- **Fix: cross-trim-level warm-starting, shell-orchestrated.** `1211` gained a `par_init` CLI override (moment set A) and prints `FINAL_PAR:...` at the end. New `Code/Deconvolution/run-trim-sequential.sh` loops trim levels in increasing order, capturing each run's `FINAL_PAR:` line and passing it as the next level's `par_init`. Dramatically more reliable (smooth, monotonic $\hat L_n(\lambda)$ within most trim levels) and much faster (good warm starts need far fewer iterations — a full 10-level chain finished in the time one or two levels took before).
- **Machine split, "overtake" mechanic:** one instrument per machine, whichever finishes first takes over the other's remaining levels. See `reference_macbook_ssh` memory for the concurrency lesson (2 jobs at 5+ cores beat 4 jobs at 3 cores badly, due to contention).
- **Grid: 10 trim levels, dense 0-5% / sparse 5-10%, capped at 10% by design** — 0.05%, 0.1%, 0.25%, 0.5%, 0.75%, 1%, 2.5%, 5%, 7%, 10%, plus the already-resolved 0%. Same 16-point log-spaced $\lambda$ grid (1e-9 to 1e-4) per level.
- **Reading the result required checking individual grid points, not just the summary CI.** At trim=10% (`lag_m`), only one isolated $\lambda$ passes with neighbors 8-10x worse on both sides — a smoking-gun optimizer artifact, don't trust it. Trim=0.05% is a real 7-point plateau. Trim=2.5%/5%: the point estimate sits at the upper edge of a wide, still-partially-open plateau — real on the upper side, lower bound not cleanly resolved at these levels.
- **The upper CI bound across trim levels is a clean, trustworthy signal once isolated-spike artifacts are excluded.** Stabilizes from ~0.25-1% trim through at least 7%, sitting in a tight $2\times10^{-6}$ to $5\times10^{-6}$ band for both instruments.
- **Firm/industry composition sanity check:** at 1% trim, 291 firm-periods / 67 distinct plants, $M^*$ ranging 57x to 3,600x the median firm. Heavily concentrated in food processing (SIC 311, ~32-48%) plus other materials-intensive manufacturing — economically unsurprising.
- **Tax-rate sanity check:** trimmed firms have low, right-skewed $\tau_P$ (median 1.9% at 1% trim); large firms ~4x over-represented among zero-rate corner firms (38% of top 1% by $M^*$ vs. 10.3% overall) — connects to the earlier finding that corner firms cluster in genuinely VAT-exempt goods, plausibly large food processors consuming huge volumes of exempt agricultural inputs. Confirms the trim mechanism targets the right population.
- **Decision: trim at 1%.** $\hat\theta$ for the counterfactual should be taken from a 1%-trimmed moment-set-A fit.

**Bug found while building the trim-cutoff figure: `fit_one_lambda_A` had no BOBYQA convergence controls at all (2026-09-05).** Unlike B (given controls on 2026-09-01), A called `nloptr::bobyqa` with defaults — fine for every combination tried up through this point, but a new (small trim, very small $\lambda$, numerically delicate) combination ran 70+ CPU-minutes on one grid point without finishing. Fixed by giving A the same controls as B (`xtol_rel=1e-4`, `maxeval=2000`, `maxtime=300`), threaded through the same CLI args. Verified: previously-hanging point converged in 66s. No prior reported result invalidated.

**Concave-$q$ alternative tested and rejected as a fix, decisively (2026-09-05, same session) — linear $q$ + trimming is the path forward, not a functional-form change.** Ran a properly-inverted diagnostic: a single continuous warm-started chain (the same warm-start-path-dependence trap recurred here — fixed the same way) from just below linear's untrimmed bound through the highest trim-implied bound ($10^{-9}$ to $2\times10^{-6}$, 23 points), on the **untrimmed** sample.
- `h_of_e_concave(e,\tau_P\rho,\lambda)=\ln(\tau_P\rho)-\lambda e+\ln(1-\lambda e)$, ceiling $\lambda e<1$ (vs. linear's $<\tfrac12$). New isolated export `mh_tilted_average_A_concave_cpp`, standalone driver `Code/Deconvolution/1220-stage2-concave-diag.R`.
- **Result, both instruments:** largest not-rejected $\lambda$ under concave $q$ is $3.5\times10^{-8}$ (lag_m) / $4.0\times10^{-8}$ (lag_2_cal_W) — only a 1.2–1.7x improvement over linear's own untrimmed bounds, nowhere near the trim-implied bounds. Past ~$4\times10^{-8}$, sharply and durably rejected.
- **Decision, per the user's own pre-committed rule:** keep linear $q$, resolve via trimming.
- **Why this makes sense:** concave's $h(e)$ only delays the ceiling blow-up to $\lambda e=1$ from linear's $\lambda e=\tfrac12$ — a fixed 2x relief factor by construction, matching the observed 1.2–1.7x shift. Trimming removes the extreme observations outright, not capped at any fixed ratio — structurally why it delivers 5–50x instead of ~2x. Power/convex $q$ was not tested (would only tighten the ceiling further, the wrong direction) and is now deprioritized for this puzzle.

**Moment system upgrade: add $h'\cdot\varepsilon$ (set A) and $h'\cdot(\ln M-\mu_{m,j})$ (set B only), drop `eps*e` — agreed, then implemented with a scaling bug found and a fix agreed (2026-09-05/06).**
- $h'\equiv\partial h/\partial\lambda$ (linear $q$: $h'=\frac{-2e}{1-2\lambda e}$) is the Schennach-style score moment for $\lambda$ — the existing system already has this structure for every other smooth parameter (`psi`, `psi*om`, `psi*om^2` are the score moments for $\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2$) without anyone naming it that way; $\lambda$ was the one missing its own. $h'\cdot\varepsilon$ closes the gap validly (not tautologically): $\varepsilon\perp(\psi,\omega)$ is already maintained, and since $e$ (hence $h'$) is a deterministic function of $(\psi,\omega)$ alone, this implies $\varepsilon\perp h'$. $h'\cdot(\ln M-\mu_{m,j})$ (B only) is structurally identical to the already-valid `psi*(lnM-mu_m,j)`, adds exactly one pooled row. `eps*e` dropped (least $\lambda$-informative of the existing rows) to make room.
- **Ruled out:** $h'\cdot\omega$ (tautological — $\omega$ is a direct argument of $e^*$, same error class as the rejected `psi*e`); $h'\cdot k$/$h'\cdot l$ directly (redundant with $h'\cdot\ln M$); a "score for $\eta$" (doesn't exist in this form — $\eta$ enters via a sampler support restriction, not smoothly through $h(e)$).
- **Parked:** $h'$ interacted with the stage-1 AR(1) instruments — unclear which independence assumption would license it. Floated, not decided: second-order versions $h'\cdot(\varepsilon^2-\sigma^2_{\varepsilon,j})$, $h'\cdot(\ln M-\mu_{m,j})^2$.
- **Implemented, both `.cpp` files compile clean, moment set A run-tested — but the raw (untransformed) $h'$ has a real numerical scaling bug, found before trusting any result.** A first-pass 16-point run at trim=1% (lag_m), seeded from the already-known trim=1% fit under the old 8-moment system, produced suspiciously exact-zero $\hat L_n$ values (2e-12, 1.4e-13, 5e-17) at the high end of the grid. Diagnosed: at $\lambda=10^{-5}$, `diag(Omega)` showed row 6 (`h_prime*eps`) with variance $1.16\times10^{16}$ — ten orders of magnitude larger than every other row. `cue_objective_from_moments`'s relative eigenvalue truncation discards anything below `max(eig)*1e-10` — with row 6 inflating `max(eig)` this much, only the top 2 of 8 eigenvector directions survived, so the near-zero $\hat L_n$ reflected BOBYQA satisfying one numerically-dominant direction, not the whole system.
- **Why raw $h'$ blows up:** $h'$ diverges as $e\to(\frac1{2\lambda})^-$ for a *given* trial $\lambda$ — the same ceiling mechanism behind the whole extreme-evader investigation, now showing up numerically. Even after 1% trimming, some remaining firm's $e$ can land close enough to whatever $\lambda$ is currently being tested. The objective's own search does not naturally avoid this — the eigenvalue-truncation artifact made the danger zone look spuriously *good*, actively drawing BOBYQA toward it.
- **Fix agreed, not yet implemented at the time of this entry: bound $h'$ via $\exp(h')$** (or $c^{h'}$ for any base $c>1$) before forming the moment — still validly independent of $\varepsilon$ under the same argument, since validity never depended on $h'$'s specific functional form, only on it being *some* function of $e$ alone. Chose $\exp$ over a signed-log alternative since $h'<0$ always makes $\exp(h')\in(0,1)$ automatically, monotonic, no reference scale needed. **Theoretical justification for using *any* transform, not just monotonic ones:** (i) full-independence route — $E[f(e)\cdot\varepsilon]=E[f(e)]\cdot0=0$ for any $f$, given $\varepsilon\perp(\psi,\omega)$ and $E[\varepsilon]=0$; (ii) weaker conditional-mean route — $E[\varepsilon|e]=0\Rightarrow E[\varepsilon|h'(e)]=0$ by the tower property for *any* function of $e$. Monotonicity only matters for informativeness, not validity. Closest textbook precedent: Huber's M-estimation (Huber 1964) — standard practice in robust-GMM, not something Schennach's or AK2020's own moments needed since theirs don't hit this kind of derivative-driven blow-up.
- **Status at the time of this entry:** not yet implemented. The trim=1% product file with raw $h'$ is broken, do not use; the pre-$h'$ 8-moment trim=1% result is preserved with a `.bak-old8moments` suffix.

## Counterfactual

**Reparametrized as a bounded percentage shift, and refit into the AK2020 test-inversion template (2026-09-04).** Instead of a scalar $\tilde\tau$, define $\Delta\in[-0.5,1]$ (50% cut to 100% increase), benefit shifter $\ln((1+\Delta)\rho_t\tau_P)$. Revised plan: grid $(\Delta,R)$ jointly (mirroring AK2020 Appendix F's $(kap,bshare)$ grid), $R$ as an auxiliary parameter with its own moment, profiling out everything else ($\lambda,\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2,\sigma^2_\psi,\eta$, per-industry nuisance, $\gamma$) at each cell — demoting $\lambda$ etc. to nuisance for this exercise specifically, not a retreat from wanting point/set estimates of $\theta$ eventually (the coordinate-wise grid-$\lambda$-alone plan becomes the natural next step after). On moment count: Nail suggested trimming, but Schennach's own text argues the opposite (ELVIS.pdf: "adding more moment conditions leads to substantial reductions in the uncertainty... requires no extra analytical work") — plan is to keep growing the moment set as identified, not preemptively cut.

**Sequencing revised, revenue formula corrected for the two-tax-rate model (2026-09-05).**
- **Phase 0 first: finish the $\lambda$ fit via test-inversion before building the counterfactual.** Extend the $\lambda$ grid until $\hat L_n(\lambda)$ genuinely turns around, then finish `1215-stage2-profile-CI-explore.R`'s sketch: $\hat Q_n(\lambda)=\hat L_n(\lambda)-\min_\lambda\hat L_n(\lambda)$, $TS(\lambda)=2n\hat Q_n(\lambda)$ vs. $\chi^2_{8,0.95}$ — giving a genuine CI for $\lambda$ itself, reusing `fit_one_lambda_A`'s existing structure unchanged. Phase 1's $(\Delta,R)$ grid then fixes $\hat\theta$ at this refined fit's argmin.
- **Revenue formula corrected: $\tau_S$ enters and must be netted against $\tau_P$, even though only $\tau_P$ is shocked by $\Delta$.** The version implicit in `9999-elvis.qmd` ($R_i(\tilde\tau)=\tilde\tau[\ldots]$, a single rate on the whole base) predates the two-tax-rate derivation and is wrong once $\tau_S\ne\tau_P$ is taken seriously. Government VAT revenue is sales-side tax collected minus the purchases-side credit claimed, and only the credit side is exposed to evasion:
$$R_i(\Delta;M_i)=\underbrace{t1_{it}}_{\tau_{S,it}\cdot P_tY_{it},\text{ fixed}}-\underbrace{\tilde\tau_{P,it}\Big[M_i+\big(1-q(\tilde e_i(\Delta))\big)\tilde e_i(\Delta)\Big]}_{\text{shocked by }\Delta},\qquad \tilde\tau_{P,it}\equiv(1+\Delta)\tau_{P,it}$$
$t1$ (raw "sales tax paid on sales" column) used directly since $\tau_S$ never changes here. Corner firms: $R_i=t1_{it}-\tilde\tau_{P,it}M^*_{it}$, no detection term. K/L don't enter (VAT-exempt). **Units:** all nominal COP — never substitute the real, log-scale `m`/`y` columns. Deflate once at aggregation ($R_i^{\text{real}}(\Delta)=R_i(\Delta)/P_t$), not mid-formula, since the panel spans a decade of inflation. **Not yet implemented at the time of this entry** — `t1`/`p_gdp_new` still need adding to `stage2_data`'s join.

**Phase 0 finished: the $\lambda$ grid was extended down to $10^{-12}$; test-inversion resolves an upper bound but not a lower one (2026-09-05).** Moment set A, both `ins`, `n_burn=500,n_keep=1000`. Extending stepwise (coarse scan → dense refinement → 26-point continuous warm-start chain → a final check to $10^{-12}$) surfaced a real methodological trap: **warm-start path dependence is large enough to flip conclusions** — the same $\lambda$ fit via two different warm-start chains gave $\hat L_n$ differing by 3x. A single continuous chain across the whole region (not disjoint patches) was necessary. `1218-stage2-lambda-ci.R`'s merge step dedupes every fit by $(\text{ins},\lambda)$, keeping the lowest $\hat L_n$ found by any chain, precisely because of this.
- **Result:** both instruments converge on nearly the same $\hat\lambda\approx1.8\times10^{-8}$ (lag_m: $1.836\times10^{-8}$, $\hat L_n=2.99\times10^{-5}$; lag_2_cal_W: $1.800\times10^{-8}$, $\hat L_n=3.70\times10^{-5}$) — a genuinely different regime, 2+ orders of magnitude better fit than anything on the original grid. **Upper 95% CI bound is real:** lag_m $2.14\times10^{-8}$, lag_2_cal_W $3.40\times10^{-8}$. **Lower bound never resolves**, even at $\lambda=10^{-12}$ — $\hat L_n(\lambda)$ visibly plateaus rather than turning upward. Honest reading at the time: this specification (linear $q$, moment set A, untrimmed) only identifies an upper bound on $\lambda$.
- **Diagnosed live: extreme evaders drive the flat plateau.** $e$'s pooled distribution is extremely right-skewed. Under linear $q$, $h(e)$ blows up as $2\lambda e\to1$ — only a handful of extreme evaders are sensitive to $\lambda$ near the ceiling; typical (small-$e$) firms have $h(e)\approx\ln(\tau_P\rho)$ regardless of $\lambda$.
