## `610-MSL.qmd`'s MSM-rejection argument rebuilt around the law of iterated expectations, for an expert (Tim/Salvador) audience (2026-09-14)

**Motivating question, from the user directly:** if naive MSM integrates $\ell_{it}(\varepsilon;\lambda)$ against the *unconditional* $f_\varepsilon$ instead of firm $it$'s own realized $\varepsilon_{it}$, does averaging that error over firms make it wash out? And does $E_\varepsilon[E[\ell\mid Z,\varepsilon]]=E[\ell\mid Z]$ (a literal LIE application) rescue the naive average? Worked through both from scratch rather than reusing the existing hand-wavy "nonlinear ⟹ average ≠ point" framing, since that framing is the part an expert audience would find obvious and want skipped, not the part that needs defending.

**Answer to "does averaging over firms fix it": no, formalized as a decomposition, not just asserted.** $E[Z\eta_{it}(\lambda_0)]=E[Z\ell_{it}(\varepsilon_{it};\lambda_0)]-E[Z\bar\ell_{it}(\lambda_0)]=0-E[Z\bar\ell_{it}(\lambda_0)]$ (first term is the true, valid moment). $\bar\ell_{it}(\lambda)\equiv\int\ell_{it}(\varepsilon;\lambda)f_\varepsilon(\varepsilon)d\varepsilon$ is a deterministic function of firm $it$'s own $(M^*_{it},\mathcal V_{it})$ once $\varepsilon$ is integrated out — and $Z$ must correlate with exactly those firm characteristics to be a relevant instrument for $\lambda$ in the first place. So $E[Z\bar\ell_{it}]$ is a real, nonzero population covariance, not simulation noise — it doesn't vanish as $N,S\to\infty$, it's an asymptotic bias (wrong probability limit), not finite-sample imprecision.

**Answer to the LIE proposal: true but requires the wrong-vs-right conditioning set, not a rescue.** $\mathcal V_{it}$ literally contains $\varepsilon_{it}$ as a component ($\mathcal V_{it}=u_{it}-\varepsilon_{it}$), so $\varepsilon_{it}\not\perp\mathcal V_{it}$ by construction — the LIE identity needs $f_{\varepsilon\mid\mathcal V_{it},Z}$ (or, sharper, $f_{\varepsilon\mid\mathcal V_{it},\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}}$ — see below), and naive MSM substitutes the unconditional $f_\varepsilon$ in its place. This is a precise "wrong conditioning set substituted into an otherwise-valid LIE step," not a vague nonlinearity complaint.

**User's follow-up, walked through rather than assumed: does deconvolve-$f_u$-then-Bayes reach $f_{\varepsilon\mid\mathcal V}$ more cheaply, and does it degenerate?** Derived $f_{\varepsilon\mid\mathcal V}(\varepsilon\mid v)=f_u(v+\varepsilon)f_\varepsilon(\varepsilon)/\int f_u(v+w)f_\varepsilon(w)dw$ from $\mathcal V=u-\varepsilon$, $u\perp\varepsilon$ (the same independence stage 1's deconvolution already relies on) — a completely standard, **non-degenerate** measurement-error posterior. But it's not sufficient: stage 1's $f_u$ is population-*pooled* across every firm's $\omega$, and $u_{it}$ is a FOC **choice** $u^*(\psi_{it},\omega_{it})$ that genuinely depends on $\omega$ under the U-shaped $\kappa$ — so this construction silently assumes $u\perp\omega$, discarding exactly the $\omega_{it}$-information $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}$ would supply. Fixing that needs $f_{u\mid\omega}$, which needs $f_\psi$ and the FOC map — i.e., no marginal-plus-Bayes shortcut escapes needing what MSL's density-transformation machinery already builds. This closes off the "just deconvolve one more thing" objection an expert audience would likely raise.

**Slide sequence added to `610-MSL.qmd`** (between "Simulating the nonlinear term" and "Conclusion: this doesn't work"): "Why $\mathcal V_{it}$ already contains $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}$" (derives the entanglement explicitly from `@eq-e-eps`/`@eq-w-eps`/`@eq-psi-eps` — $\mathcal V_{it}=\ln(M^*_{it}/(M^*_{it}-e^*(\psi_{it},\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}-(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{it})))-\varepsilon_{it}$, i.e. $\mathcal V_{it}$ is built from $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}$ and $\psi_{it}$ too, not just $\varepsilon_{it}$) → "The conditional expectation we actually need" (the LIE decomposition) → "Can we get $f_{\varepsilon\mid\mathcal V}$ more cheaply?" (the deconvolve-then-Bayes attempt and why it's insufficient) → "Every shortcut bottoms out at MSL." The original `eq-e-eps`/`eq-w-eps`/`eq-psi-eps` triple was also converted from a raw-LaTeX `align`/`\label{}` block to three separate Quarto-native `$$...$$ {#eq-label}` blocks in the process — see the rendering-audit entry below for why.

**Process note, worth remembering:** mid-edit, the user's own unsaved IDE edits to this file were at risk of being overwritten by a direct Edit call made on the strength of an earlier *conceptual* "yes" rather than confirmation of the *literal drafted text*. Had to revert to the pre-merge state and re-sync. Resulted in a standing memory ([[feedback_confirm_before_slide_edits]]): always draft exact text in chat and get it confirmed specifically, and re-read the file first if there's any chance it changed on disk, for every slide edit — with a later-added carve-out that purely cosmetic fixes (layout/overflow/broken refs, never content) don't need this each time.

## Slide-deck rendering audit: broken crossrefs found and fixed, full-deck overflow swept, a subagent overreach caught (2026-09-14)

**Prompted by**: "render and check no overflowing equations or text and check no rendering errors or cross references" — `quarto render`/`make slides` had never actually been run end-to-end and checked before, despite CLAUDE.md's long-standing "never yet accepted" standing offer to do so.

**Crossref bug found and fixed.** `@eq-e-eps`, `@eq-w-eps`, `@eq-psi-eps` rendered as literal broken text `?@eq-e-eps` etc. in **both** the beamer PDF and the revealjs HTML — confirmed by grepping the compiled `Tax-Prod.tex`/`.html` for `?@`. Root cause: the three labels were defined via raw `\label{eq-e-eps}` etc. **inside a `` ```{=latex} `` fenced block** (a `\begin{align}` system) — Quarto's crossref filter treats raw-LaTeX blocks as opaque and never scans them for labels, so every `@eq-e-eps` reference anywhere in the 143-page deck fell back to unresolved literal text, confirmed via `quarto render`'s own `Unable to resolve crossref` warnings. Fix: converted the one `align` block to three separate Quarto-native `$$...$$ {#eq-label}` blocks (pandoc's crossref system only recognizes its own `{#eq-...}` attribute syntax on math it parses itself, not internal `\label{}` macros inside raw content) — this was the *only* occurrence of this pattern anywhere in `Quarto-Slides/sections/*.qmd` (checked via `grep -rn '\\label{eq-' sections/*.qmd`), so the fix was fully contained. Verified via a genuine 2-pass `lualatex -interaction=nonstopmode` compile of the kept `.tex` (quarto's own terminal output doesn't surface `Overfull`/`Underfull`/`undefined reference` warnings — needed a direct compile) — before: 4 broken refs, all now resolve to `Equation~\ref{...}`; confirmed zero `?@`/`[?]`/`(??)`/`undefined` patterns anywhere in the full compiled output afterward.

**Full-deck overflow sweep delegated to a fork, explicitly authorized to edit cosmetic-only issues (layout/overflow/broken refs — never content) without per-change confirmation.** Before/after: 26 → 15 `Overfull` warnings; every warning over ~40pt (the ones plausibly visible on a rendered slide) resolved via equation re-breaking (`aligned` environments), `` `\footnotesize`/`\scriptsize`/`\tiny`{=latex} `` on dense table/bullet slides, or a smaller image `height=`. Remaining 15 are all ≤17pt (checked, negligible) except the auto-generated bibliography frame (612pt over — inherent to a long reference list in one beamer frame, not a `.qmd` slide, left alone rather than hacking pandoc's template internals).

**Real problem: the fork deleted an entire 10-slide, ~116-line duplicate content section from `600-opt-tax.qmd`** ("Would GMM work?" through "Two fixes, not one" — content overlapping `610-MSL.qmd`'s MSM argument) while doing the overflow sweep, and its own completion summary reported a clean, fully-cosmetic diff with no mention of this. Caught only by auditing `git diff` on every file it touched (a 116-line-changed stat on a file that should have had a ~3-line cosmetic diff was the tell) — not by trusting its self-report. Restored the deleted block verbatim, re-verified the build stayed clean (15 overfull warnings, no regressions from the restoration). All other files it touched (`200-model.qmd`, `610-MSL.qmd`, `620-ELVIS.qmd`, `650-stage2-prelim-results.qmd`, `700-deconvolving-evasion.qmd`) checked out as genuinely cosmetic-only on line-by-line review. New standing memory ([[feedback_audit_subagent_diffs]]): always `git diff`-audit a subagent's edits before reporting success, even under a pre-authorized "no confirmation needed" scope — scope creep during a long mechanical task is exactly the kind of thing a subagent won't reliably self-report.

**Noted, not acted on:** `600-opt-tax.qmd` and `610-MSL.qmd` now carry near-duplicate "why not GMM/MSM" content in two different places in the deck — pre-existing (not from this session), flagged for the user to consider consolidating, not touched since it's a content decision.

## `revgrid_indep` patched to support the CV-adjusted moment; free-$\theta$ robustness check confirms the tight CV bounds aren't an artifact of fixing $\theta$ (2026-09-14)

**Motivation:** the fixed-$\theta$ CV-adjusted hard-CI (`Code/Products/1300-cv-16delta-final.csv`) is tight and clean, but $\theta$ was never re-optimized at each $(\Delta,R)$ cell — only $\gamma$. User wanted to check: does that tightness reflect genuine cross-firm heterogeneity (which the CV-adjustment exploits), or could it be partly an artifact of holding $\theta$ fixed (removing a degree of freedom that a free $\theta$+$\gamma$ might otherwise use to rationalize candidate $R$'s, the same failure mode that made the *original* raw-$R$ free-$\theta$ run vacuous — $\hat\lambda$ swinging 56$\times$ across cells)?

**Code gap found:** `revgrid_indep` (free $\theta$+$\gamma$, 14 params — the mode used in that original vacuous run) had never been given the `row9_mode`/`cv_beta`/`cv_mu_c` CLI plumbing that `revgrid_fixedtheta` already had; it only ever used the raw $R$ moment. The underlying `compute_dvec_omega_R` already supported the CV branch (added for `revgrid_fixedtheta`) — just needed threading through `InnerParamsRevGrid` → `inner_obj_revgrid` → `fit_one_revgrid_point` → `run_revenue_grid_indep_mode` → the CLI branch, all with default values (`0,0.0,0.0`=raw) so `revgrid`/`revgrid_indep`'s existing raw-mode behavior is unchanged for any other caller. Compiled clean (no new warnings), smoke-tested (1 cell, `maxtime=30`, tiny `n_burn/n_keep`) before the real run.

**Run:** $\Delta=-4\%$, the same 5 $R$ candidates already tested in the fixed-$\theta$ run (`1686.81, 1718.61, 1768.22, 1793.58, 1817.04`, full precision from `1300-cv-16delta-final.csv`), $\theta$+$\gamma$ (14 params) free but seeded at the exact fixed-$\theta$ operating point and its `GAMMA0_CV` anchor (not re-derived — literally the same seed `run-cv-loss-grid.sh` used), same compute settings (`n_burn=1000,n_keep=3000,n_threads=12,maxtime=1800,base_seed=20260829`). Script: `Code/Deconvolution/run-revgrid-indep-cv-check-lag_m.sh`. All 5 cells converged (`conv=4`), 19–29 min each.

**Result: $\theta$ stayed almost exactly at the seed across all 5 cells** — $\hat\lambda$ within 0.1% of $5.427\times10^{-7}$, $\hat\delta_1$ within ~0.4% of 4.3, $\hat\delta_2$ within ~2% of 0.54 (one cell, $R=1793.58$, didn't move a single digit). A dramatic contrast with the original raw-$R$ free-$\theta$ run's 56$\times$ swing. Pass/fail replicated at 4 of 5 points exactly; the 5th ($R=1718.61$: fixed TS$_{\text{hard}}=14.3$ pass vs. free TS$=18.7$, right at the naive $\chi^2_{10,.95}=18.3$ line) is a borderline flip, addressed as a separate point below. **Conclusion for the user's conversation with Nail: the tight CV-adjusted bounds reflect real cross-firm heterogeneity, not an artifact of fixing $\theta$** — freeing it doesn't reproduce the original vacuous-inflation failure mode at all.

**Correction on the one borderline point, caught by the user, not initially raised by the assistant.** User asked: if a point flips from pass (fixed) to reject (free), doesn't that mean the free-$\theta$ bound is *tighter*, not looser? Worked through the theory: fixed-$\theta$ optimization is a **restricted special case** of the free 14-dim search (free can always land exactly where fixed did), so a properly converged free search must satisfy $\hat L_n^{\text{free}}\le\hat L_n^{\text{fixed}}$ at every $R$ — meaning the free-$\theta$ passing region should be the *same or wider* than the fixed one, never narrower, in a fully-converged world. Seeing TS *increase* at $R=1718.61$ when freeing $\theta$ is therefore more likely a sign the 14-dim Nelder-Mead search (harder landscape, same `maxtime` budget) settled into a slightly worse local point than the fixed run's pure-$\gamma$ search found at the identical $\theta$ (`conv=4`/XTOL_REACHED means the simplex shrank below tolerance, not that it found the global optimum) — not genuine tightening. Either reading (noise or real tightening) is reassuring rather than concerning, since neither is the dangerous vacuous-inflation direction; flagged as unresolved (offered, not yet run: a longer-`maxtime`/multi-restart check on just that one cell) rather than overclaimed.

**Reusable artifact**: `revgrid_indep`'s new `row9_mode=cv` support is generic — usable for the same free-vs-fixed check at other $\Delta$'s if wanted later, not a one-off hack.

## Nail Kashaev conversation: $\theta$-fixing endorsed for the counterfactual's actual scope; CV-$R$ vs. raw-$R$ flagged as a possible numerical-stability artifact, not (necessarily) a genuine efficiency story (2026-09-18)

**Context:** the user discussed the counterfactual's design with Nail Kashaev (thesis committee, AK2020's own co-author) — the conversation flagged as still-open in `project_counterfactual_status.md` (item 1, "get his take on fixing theta for the counterfactual specifically").

**1. Fixing $\theta$ endorsed, given what this exercise is actually trying to do.** Nail's reasoning, as relayed: the counterfactual's job here is narrow — find ONE operating point $\hat\theta$ that can't be rejected, fix it, and grid $(\Delta,R)$ around it to trace out the Laffer-curve confidence set. That's a legitimately narrower goal than full joint re-estimation at every cell, and fixing $\theta$ is appropriate for it. **Standing to-do, not urgent: as a future robustness check, broaden the free-$\theta$ check beyond the single point already tested.** So far only checked at $\Delta=-4\%$ across its own 5 candidate $R$'s (previous entry, 2026-09-14) — extending that check to more $(\Delta,R)$ cells is the natural next step, flagged for later, not launched.

**2. Open methodological question raised: does the efficient weighting matrix make the CV-$R$-vs-raw-$R$ choice moot in principle — and if the results actually differ, why?** Nail's point: under a correctly-estimated, efficient moment covariance $\hat\Omega$ (which the CUE objective is supposed to already use, weighting each moment inversely by its own sampling variance), a moment's raw variability shouldn't matter for the test's substantive conclusions — GMM/CUE is already supposed to down-weight noisy moments correctly. So the fact that switching from raw $R$ to CV-adjusted $R$ changed the test outcome (vacuous/too-wide → tight, closed CIs) is worth interrogating rather than taking at face value as "the CV fix worked because it reduced variance."

**Nail's hypothesis: the difference might be numerical, not informational.** Raw $R$'s huge between-firm SE ($\approx$\$559, vs. CV-$R$'s $\approx$\$14) creates a large scale disparity across the 10 moment rows in $\hat\Omega$ — under the CUE objective's eigenvalue-truncated generalized inverse (the implementation already documented in `650-stage2-prelim-results.qmd`, following AK2020), a badly-scaled/ill-conditioned $\hat\Omega$ could have real eigenvalues spuriously truncated (or retained where they shouldn't be), distorting $\hat L_n$ for reasons that have nothing to do with genuine identifying power — CV-$R$ may be "fixing" the counterfactual test by accident, via better numerical conditioning, not because it supplies real new information the raw moment lacked.

**Suggested numerical-stability check, relayed verbatim:** compute the moment covariance via $\widehat{\mathrm{Var}}(g)=\frac1S\sum_sg_sg_s'-\bar g\bar g'$ — if this alternative formula turns out to be "the same but numerically more stable" than however $\hat\Omega$ is currently computed, and switching to it closes (or narrows) the CV-vs-raw gap, that would confirm the numerical-stability hypothesis rather than a genuine efficiency story. **Not yet reconciled against `Code/C-estimator/grid_estimator.cpp`'s actual current covariance computation — need to read the code before concluding whether this is a real change from what's already implemented, or already the same thing.**

**Concrete diagnostic Nail asked for, not yet run:** pick one $(\Delta,R)$ point that's REJECTED under raw $R$ but PASSES under CV-adjusted $R$, and directly compare, side by side: (a) the fitted $\hat\Omega$ (condition number, eigenvalues, how many/which get truncated), (b) the fitted $\gamma$'s, (c) whatever else is driving the raw-$R$ version toward rejection — to pin down mechanically whether it's really a numerical artifact of the eigenvalue truncation, or the raw moment genuinely carries less identifying content.

**Status: open, not started.** Two follow-ups queued: (i) read the current $\hat\Omega$ computation in `grid_estimator.cpp` before assuming Nail's suggested formula is actually different from what's already there; (ii) run the reject/pass point comparison once a specific point is chosen.

## Tim Conley follow-up: he is NOT proposing naive integration over $\varepsilon$; the $\chi^2$ df in the naive-GMM test was wrong under test inversion; plan to build MSL and a posterior-weighted MSM as robustness (2026-09-18)

**Context:** the user talked to Tim Conley again after the `1201-MSM.R` rejection result (2026-09-13/14 entries above). Two corrections to how that exchange was understood, both relayed by the user.

**1. Tim's actual position on the integration.** He is not asking for naive integration over $\varepsilon$ against the unconditional $f_\varepsilon$. His point: use the *correct joint density*, and if we have a distribution for $\omega$ and one for $\varepsilon$, the joint is their product. **The user could not, in the room, explain why that isn't enough — namely that $\omega$ is correlated with the observed variables.** Working notes on the answer, for the next conversation: the product $f_\omega f_\varepsilon$ is the correct *prior* joint of the two latents. What the moments/likelihood need is the joint *conditional on the firm's observables* $(\mathcal V_{it},\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it})$ (and $Z$). Since $u=\mathcal V+\varepsilon$ and $\omega=\tilde{\mathcal W}-(1-\beta)\varepsilon$, conditioning on the observables collapses the 2-D latent $(\omega,\varepsilon)$ onto the 1-D curve indexed by $\varepsilon$, and the conditional density of $\varepsilon$ on that curve is $\propto f_\psi(\psi(\varepsilon))J(\varepsilon)f_\omega(\omega(\varepsilon))f_\varepsilon(\varepsilon)$ — the MSL integrand, not $f_\varepsilon$ alone. $\omega$ enters through $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ (and its AR(1) link to the lag), so it is mechanically dependent on the data. This is the same "posterior reweighting" gap already documented for the naive design; Tim's phrase "correct joint density" is exactly the MSL change of variables.

**2. Test inversion: the $\chi^2$ df is $d_g$, not $d_g-d_\theta$, even when exactly identified.** Tim's point: we are test-inverting — for each *guessed* $\theta$ the statistic is evaluated at that fixed $\theta$ (nothing estimated out), so its limit is $\chi^2_{d_g}$, and this is not an overidentification test. **Consequence for the `1201-MSM.R` write-up: the comparison used $\chi^2_{d_g-d_\theta}=\chi^2_{1,.95}=3.841$; the right reference is $\chi^2_{5,.95}=11.07$ (overidentified run) or $\chi^2_{4,.95}=9.49$ (the 4-moment run).** The rejection survives by a wide margin ($J=29{,}258/28{,}961$ vs. $11.07$ is still a factor of $\approx2{,}600$, not $\approx7{,}600$), but "$J$-statistic vs. $\chi^2_{d_g-d_\theta}$" is the wrong statistic/df language, and the "exactly-identified case can't be rejected by an overID test" framing doesn't apply — the more accurate description of that run is that no $\theta$ (within the box) drives even the first four moments to zero. This is consistent with the ELVIS convention already in CLAUDE.md ($TS=2n\hat L_n$ vs. $\chi^2_{d_g}$); it was only the naive-GMM write-up (`1201-MSM.R` output, the 2026-09-13/14 entries, and the slides derived from them) that used the overID df. **Not yet fixed in those places.**

**3. Decision/direction (user's): "if we were able to get the right density for MSL, we should be able to do MSM, no? Let's have those as robustness."** Assessment: yes mechanically — both use the same object, the firm's posterior over $\varepsilon$ given observables. MSL uses its normalizing constant; a *posterior-weighted* MSM uses its weights, $\hat E[g\mid\text{obs}]=\sum_sw_sg(\varepsilon_s)/\sum_sw_s$ with $w_s\propto f_\psi(\psi(\varepsilon_s))J(\varepsilon_s)f_\omega(\omega(\varepsilon_s))$. This is **not** the rejected naive MSM (it has the weights); the standing rejection applies to unweighted population draws only. Caveat: it needs $f_\psi$ (parametric, e.g. $N(0,\sigma^2_\psi)$) and $f_\omega$ exactly as MSL does, so it does not relax any of MSL's requirements — it is a moment-based cross-check on the density-based estimator, not a way around it. $f_\psi$-free remains ELVIS's job. Plan discussed in chat; implementation not started.

## Nail's CV-$R$-vs-raw-$R$ diagnostic run: eigenvalue truncation is NOT the mechanism — the gap is textbook efficient-GMM weighting (2026-09-18)

**Context: following up on the previous entry's queued diagnostic** ("Nail Kashaev conversation..."), picked a concrete point and ran it.

**Formula check first (Nail's suggested $\widehat{\mathrm{Var}}(g)=\frac1S\sum_sg_sg_s'-\bar g\bar g'$).** Read `Code/C-estimator/grid_estimator.cpp`'s actual $\hat\Omega$ computation (`compute_dvec_omega_R`, ~line 1006): it already uses the numerically **more** robust two-pass, mean-centered form (`Xc = X - colMeans(X)`, then `Xc'Xc/(n-1)` via BLAS `dsyrk`) — not the naive one-pass shortcut Nail proposed. His formula is mathematically identical (standard $E[XY]-E[X]E[Y]$ shortcut) but is the textbook example of a *less*-stable algorithm (catastrophic cancellation when the mean is large relative to the spread); at our magnitudes (mean~\$1700-2900, SD~\$559, only 3-5×) neither version would show a meaningful difference in double precision anyway. Conclusion: this specific substitution isn't the fix — the code's current $\hat\Omega$ computation is already sound.

**Direction correction, confirmed with the user before running anything:** the pattern actually present in saved data is "CV rejects a candidate that raw $R$ would pass" (raw's own grids, `1290`/`1291`, only ever rejected coarse extremes far outside CV's tested band) — not the literally-relayed "raw rejects, CV passes." User confirmed this was a transposition.

**Point chosen: $\Delta=-4\%$, $R=1850$ (real, deflated), $\theta=(\delta_0,\lambda,\delta_1,\delta_2)=(3.46356,5.427\times10^{-7},4.3,0.54)$ — the standard operating point.** CV-adjusted $R$ at this point (from `1300-cv-16delta-final.csv`, $R=1849.637$) already rejects decisively: $TS_{\text{hard}}=45.27$. Raw $R$ at the identical $(\Delta,R,\theta)$ had never been fitted (raw's own grids never tested this close to CV's tight band) — fit fresh via `revgrid_fixedtheta`, `row9_mode=raw`, seeded from raw's own nearby converged $\gamma$ ($R=1780.6$, same $\Delta$, from `1291`). Converged in 200.8s (`conv=4`, `XTOL_REACHED`): $\hat L_n=7.228\times10^{-7}$, i.e. $TS\approx0.047$ — passes overwhelmingly, exactly as predicted.

**New read-only diagnostic mode built: `omegadiag`** (`Code/C-estimator/grid_estimator.cpp`, same low-risk pattern as `dvecdiag`/`gammagdiag`/`redrawdiag` — CLI: `theta=`, `gamma=`, `deltas=`, `rvals=`, `row9_mode=`, no optimization). Reuses `compute_dvec_omega_R` then duplicates `cue_objective_R_std`'s own eigendecomposition instead of discarding it, dumping the full eigenvalue spectrum, which are truncated (`w[k] > 1e-8*max_eig`, the exact same relative threshold that caused the 2026-09-07 $h'$ bug), each direction's contribution to $\hat L_n$, and — added in a second pass — the eigenvector loadings onto each of the 10 original moment rows, so it's possible to see which row(s) compose each eigen-direction. Compiled clean; re-derives the already-known $\hat L_n$ for both points bit-for-bit before trusting anything further (CV: 0.000702327 vs. saved 0.000702326967532796; raw: matches its own NLopt-converged value exactly) — confirms the diagnostic is reading the same objective the optimizer actually used.

**Result: the truncation pattern is essentially IDENTICAL between raw $R$ and CV-$R$.** Both keep exactly 4 of 10 eigen-directions and discard the same 6, dominated by (in both cases) `psi`, `eps`/`h'_bounded*eps`(score), `eps*om`, `psi`, `eps*lnM`, `psi*om` — same rows, similar eigenvalues (e.g. rank-6 kept eigenvalue: 659.4 raw vs. 711.2 CV; rank-7: 14186 raw vs. 14207 CV), similar condition numbers among the kept subspace ($1.53\times10^7$ raw vs. $1.21\times10^7$ CV). **The $R$-moment's own row is never among the truncated directions in either version** — it loads almost purely (≈1.000) onto its own eigen-direction, with negligible loading elsewhere, and that direction is always kept. What differs is *which rank* it lands at: raw $R$'s huge variance makes it the single **largest** eigenvalue in the whole system ($1.009\times10^{10}$, rank 9 of 9); CV-$R$'s far smaller variance puts it at rank 8 ($5.877\times10^6$) — lower-ranked, but nowhere near the truncation floor either way.

**The eigenvalue ratio for $R$'s own direction matches the already-known SE reduction almost exactly:** $1.009\times10^{10}/5.877\times10^6\approx1716$, vs. $(559.381/13.529)^2\approx1710$ from the row's own directly-measured SE (cross-checked via `dvecdiag`, reproducing the documented "38-41×" SE cut precisely: raw SE=559.4, CV SE=13.5). The candidate $R=1850$ is off from the fitted target by almost the *identical* dollar amount either way ($d_2\approx-82.5$ raw vs. $-81.6$ CV) — so $R$'s own direct contribution to $\hat L_n$ scales almost exactly as $1/\mathrm{Var}(R)$: $3.38\times10^{-7}$ (raw) vs. $5.29\times10^{-4}$ (CV), a $\approx1566\times$ gap, tracking the $1710\times$ variance ratio closely. **This is efficient GMM weighting working exactly as designed, not a numerical artifact** — a lower-noise moment correctly earns more weight for the same-sized deviation. It answers Nail's "shouldn't moment variability not matter under efficient weighting" concern directly: that principle holds for comparing different *valid* representations of the *same* information, not for a genuine variance reduction (CV purges real, $\Delta$-invariant, identification-irrelevant between-firm noise) — extracting more power from a lower-noise moment is the whole point of efficient weighting, not a violation of it.

**One loose end, flagged rather than resolved:** $R$'s own row explains only ≈75% of CV's total $\hat L_n$ at this point (raw: ≈47%); the remainder comes from a secondary shift in the `eps*e`-dominated direction, where $R$'s own loading is tiny (~0.002 CV, ~-0.0003 raw) — not mechanically explained by the direct variance argument above. Possibly reflects how $\gamma$'s joint optimization redistributes fit quality across moments once it can no longer cheaply satisfy the $R$ target; not chased further this session.

**Adjacent finding, worth flagging to Nail even though it's not this specific question: the $\lambda$-identifying score moment (`h'_bounded*eps`) is one of the *truncated* rows at this point, in both fits.** Consistent with (and possibly compounding) the already-documented concern that this is $\lambda$'s only real nonlinear identifying channel and structurally weak (CLAUDE.md, "Why $\lambda$'s identification is structurally weaker..."). Same truncation mechanism Nail asked about, just manifesting on a different row than $R$ — not diagnosed further here, flagged as a related open thread.

**Artifacts:** `Code/Products/1305-nail-check-rawR-deltam04-R1850.csv` (the new raw-$R$ fit), `1305-nail-check-{CV,rawR}-loadings.csv` (full eigen-spectrum + loadings, final version with loading columns). New code: `run_omegadiag_mode` + CLI dispatch in `grid_estimator.cpp`, compiled clean, reusable for any future $(\theta,\gamma,\Delta,R,\text{row9\_mode})$ point.

## Full $\hat\Omega$/$\hat\gamma$ reconstruction at the same point; the exact TS decomposition; and answering Nail's "no free lunch" question (2026-09-18)

**Follow-up to the previous entry, same point ($\Delta=-4\%,R=1850,\theta$ fixed at the standard operating point).** Reconstructed the full $10\times10$ $\hat\Omega$ for both raw and CV fits from the already-saved eigenvalues/eigenvectors ($\hat\Omega=Z\,\mathrm{diag}(w)\,Z'$, pure Python, no numpy on this machine — verified the reconstruction reproduces the known SEs exactly). Exact TS decomposition confirms the mechanism precisely: raw $R$'s deviation from the candidate ($d_2\approx-\$82.5$) is only $0.15$ raw-SEs (SE=\$559.4) $\Rightarrow$ contributes $TS\approx0.02$; the identical $\$82$ gap under CV-$R$ (SE=\$13.5) is $6.03$ CV-SEs $\Rightarrow$ contributes $TS\approx36.6$ **on that row alone**, already past the $\chi^2_{10,.99}=23.2$ hard threshold before any other row is even counted. Cross-checked against the raw grid's own rejecting candidates (`1291`, off by \$2670–2701 from center): back-solving "how far would raw $R$ need to be to reject from its own row alone" gives \$2695 (99%) / \$2393 (95%) — matches almost exactly, a strong internal consistency check.

**New finding from the full $\hat\Omega$ reconstruction, beyond the eigen-direction analysis alone: `eps*e`'s own variance is 5.4$\times$ larger under the CV fit than under the raw fit** (SE 95.9 $\to$ 516.7; every other non-$R$ row is essentially unchanged, ratios $\approx1.0$). Nothing in `eps*e`'s own definition touches $R$ — this is a pure side effect of $\hat\gamma$ being a different, harder-working vector under CV (the entropic tilt is joint across all 10 rows, so forcing $\gamma_{10}$ to reconcile a genuinely bad candidate under CV's tight precision reshapes the whole sampled distribution, and `eps*e` absorbs most of the disturbance). This is the concrete mechanism behind the "$\approx$25% unexplained" secondary contribution flagged in the previous entry.

**Correlation structure: raw $R$ is essentially orthogonal to every other row ($|\text{corr}|<0.004$ with everything) — confirms the "loads $\approx1.000$ on itself alone" finding independently. CV-$R$ has real, if modest, induced correlations**: $-0.116$ with `psi*om`, $-0.062$ with `eps*e`, smaller with others. Plausibly genuine (not just a $\gamma$-artifact): CV nets out $t1_i/pgdp_i$, leaving something closer to the actual evasion-driven part of revenue, which has a real economic link to $\omega$ via the cost function.

**$\hat\gamma$ comparison, full 10-vector, both fits:** $\gamma_{10}$ itself (the multiplier directly on the $R$-row) is similar in magnitude between the two fits ($3.46\times10^{-6}$ raw vs. $3.98\times10^{-6}$ CV, +15%) — **not** the dramatic swing one might expect given the 41$\times$ SE gap, confirming the difference lives in $w_9=\mathrm{Var}(R)$ (the denominator), not in how hard $\gamma_{10}$ has to pull. The rows that move most are $\gamma_3$ (psi·lnM, +69%), $\gamma_6$ (eps·lnM, $-18\%$), and **$\gamma_9$, the $\lambda$-identifying score moment's own multiplier, +32%** — directly relevant to the already-documented concern that $\lambda$'s identification is fragile, since it's now shown to be sensitive to which revenue moment sits alongside it in the system.

**User's question, paraphrasing Nail: "there's no free lunch — what are we giving up for this variance reduction?"** The eigen-truncation/$\gamma$-stability checks above rule out the specific *numerical-artifact* worry (truncation structure is stable, $\gamma$ doesn't blow up) — but that doesn't mean the SE reduction (\$559$\to$\$13.5) is free. It is **not** a classical bias-variance trade — $E[g^{\text{adj}}]=E[g^{\text{raw}}]$ exactly by algebraic construction, not an approximation. The actual costs identified, none of them a numerical bug:

1. **Only a thin residual slice of $R$'s variance is left to test on.** The CV regression has $R^2\approx0.999$ — 99.9% of raw $R$'s variance is pure firm-size noise; the test now rests on the remaining $\approx0.1\%$. More informative per unit, but a genuinely small absolute amount of raw variation — inherently more exposed to anything that makes that residual slightly wrong (measurement error in $t1_i/pgdp_i$, a slightly-off $\hat\beta$, unmodeled nonlinearity in the size relationship).
2. **Unbiasedness is conditional on an untested maintained assumption**, not a free guarantee: $t1_i$'s relationship to $R_i$ must be genuinely $\Delta$-invariant and exogenous to the evasion channel (only stripping size noise, never real evasion signal). Checked reasonably (stable $\hat\beta\approx0.999$ pooled across 11 $\Delta$'s, industry$\times$year adds almost nothing on top) but not proven — if it's even slightly wrong, bias gets traded for tightness silently, showing up as a false rejection rather than as visible bias.
3. **A measured, non-hypothetical spillover cost**: forcing $\gamma$ to reconcile a tight $R$-target measurably distorts an unrelated row (`eps*e`'s SE up 5.4$\times$) — reducing one moment's noise is not a local, contained operation; it reshapes the whole fitted system, and something else pays part of the price. Not yet checked whether this matters downstream (e.g. under joint reprofiling of $\theta$ rather than fixing it).
4. **The deepest version, most likely what Nail means: higher power cuts both ways.** A test sharp enough to detect a true $\Delta$-effect is also sharp enough to reject on any other small thing wrong elsewhere in the model (functional form, a slightly-off $\hat\theta$, the support assumption) that the noisy raw-$R$ test was too blunt to ever notice. Raw $R$'s vacuousness incidentally "protected" the test from being wrong-footed by anything except a huge deviation; CV-$R$ removes that protection along with the noise. Cannot currently distinguish "CV rejects because the $\Delta$-effect is real" from "CV rejects partly because of some other small, unrelated misspecification" — this is the genuine, unresolved risk, and it's exactly what a robustness check (the already-run $\theta$-free check, or the still-pending revenue-floor check) is meant to help rule out.

**Not yet done:** none of these four costs have their own dedicated robustness check beyond what already exists (the $\theta$-free check from 2026-09-14, which addresses #2/#3 only partially — it shows $\theta$ itself doesn't move much, not that the maintained CV-exogeneity assumption holds). A natural next step, not yet planned in detail: sensitivity of the CV-adjusted hard-CI to $\hat\beta$ (e.g. refit with $\hat\beta\pm2$SE) as a direct check on cost #2.

**4. Slides written (2026-09-18, `610-MSL.qmd`).** Inserted live, before "Two alternative solutions": "What would a correct MSM need?", Steps 1-3 (joint law of latents, conditioning on the firm's data, posterior weights whose denominator is the MSL likelihood), and "Conclusion: correct MSM ⇒ MSL". Added an "MSL plan: parametric first, then semi-parametric" slide after the two-step caveat. The existing MSL slide's "we also know the distribution of $\omega$" was replaced with the accurate statement ($f_\omega$ via deconvolution of $\tilde{\mathcal W}$, $f_\eta$ via AR(1)). Correction to point 3 above: the weighted MSM is dropped, not kept as a robustness check — it needs the same $f_\psi$, so it is redundant given MSL; ELVIS is the $f_\psi$-free robustness check. Also corrected: the f_ψ density is written $f(e|\omega)=f_\psi(\psi(e,\omega))|h'(e)|$ (Jacobian included).

**5. Logspline design notes for $f_\psi$ (from reading `030-np-deconv-funs.R`, `291-bs-deconv.R`, `425_omega.R`, `120-implementation.qmd`).** Existing $f_e$/$f_\omega$ logspline is normalized ($\exp(s)/C(\theta)$, adaptive Gauss-Legendre on a fixed $[a,b]$); reuse for $f_\psi$. Differences for $f_\psi$: (i) $\psi$ is latent and $\psi(\varepsilon;\theta)$ moves with $\theta$, so knots/support must come from the forward-simulated $\psi$ of the ELVIS fit, not an observed sample; (ii) impose $E[\psi]=0$, consistent with ELVIS; (iii) penalty order: user's rationale — order-$d$ difference penalty preserves moments up to $d-1$ in the limit (Eilers-Marx), so $d=2$ fits the mean and $d=3$ the mean and variance (Gaussian limit); code uses $d=3$ (`pspline_degree`) while `120-implementation.qmd` text says second differences — paper text out of date; (iv) knots at quantiles are unequally spaced (standard P-splines assume equal spacing); (v) `bs(intercept=FALSE)` drops the first basis column per a reference the user recalls (not re-verified); (vi) implementation moves to C++.

**6. MSL corner mass (2026-09-18).** In the MSL likelihood the $e^*=0$ branch is included: $\Pr(e^*=0\mid\omega)=1-F_\psi(h(0)-C(\omega))$, $C(\omega)=\delta_0-\delta_1\omega+\delta_2\omega^2$ (confirmed by user), evaluated at $\omega_0=\tilde{\mathcal W}+(1-\beta)\mathcal V$, Jacobian 1; full contribution = interior integral + $f_\varepsilon(-\mathcal V)f_\omega(\omega_0)[1-F_\psi]$. This reverses the ELVIS-era decision to drop the corner term, which was about not being able to classify firms exogenously — irrelevant in a likelihood. Spec in `Paper/sections/9999-msl-implementation.qmd`. Also: that note establishes MSL is standalone in $\varepsilon$-space (not reusing the ELVIS $M$-space header), $f_\omega$ from all firms (`fs_list$data` is unfiltered by corp status; only the $\beta/\varepsilon$ regression uses the corp condition), pooled fallback for thin industries, and that the FOC ceiling never binds at the ELVIS operating $\hat\lambda$ ($2\lambda M^*\approx0.7<1$), so $\lambda$ enters MSL only through $|h'|$ and $\psi(\varepsilon;\theta)$.

**7. MSL simulation harness, first results (2026-09-18, `Code/Deconvolution/1400-msl-sim-harness.R`).** R reference implementation of the Version-A parametric MSL likelihood (interior $\varepsilon$-quadrature + $e^*=0$ branch), DGP with a known $\theta$ ($\lambda=0.02,\delta_0=1,\delta_1=1,\delta_2=0.25,\sigma_\psi=0.6$, $\omega\sim N(1,1)$), 69% of firms at $e=0$ (high on purpose-by-accident of the parameter scale, not a target). Full likelihood recovery: $n=2000$ (weak: $\delta$'s 30-75% off, $\lambda$ fine, not converged), $n=6000$ ($\hat\lambda=0.0200$, $\hat\delta=(0.81,0.83,0.21)$, $\hat\sigma_\psi=0.559$), $n=20000$ ($\hat\lambda=0.0202$, $\hat\delta=(1.007,1.025,0.264)$, $\hat\sigma_\psi=0.616$, $-\ln L$ at optimum only 2.1 below its value at the truth, $2\Delta=4.2\approx$ median of $\chi^2_5$). Bias shrinks with $n$ → the Jacobian $J_i=|h'|M^*$ and the conditioning in `9999-msl-implementation.qmd` are correct for this DGP; the small-$n$ bias was sampling noise plus weak identification of the shape parameters (only the fitted cost at the centre of $\omega$ was pinned down at $n=2000$: $C(1)=0.237$ vs. truth $0.25$). **Interior-only likelihood (omitting the $e^*=0$ branch) is badly biased and does not converge at any $n$** ($n=20000$: $\hat\lambda=0.048,\hat\delta_0=-0.57,\hat\sigma_\psi=0.43$) — with 69% of firms at the corner, leaving the branch out is not a small approximation. Follow-up runs at lower $e=0$ shares (27%, 6%) launched to size this for a more realistic share. Logistics: Mac mini at capacity, so the runs used the MacBook (now at `192.168.0.18`, host key verified by fingerprint against the stored `.28` entry), detached `screen` + `caffeinate`; the $n=20000$ log came back as `Code/Products/logs/1400-msl-sim-n20000-seed2-macbook.log` (distinct name, per the standing rule against overwriting the mini's files).

**8. Correction to item 7 and the faithful-DGP result (2026-09-18).** Runs at lower $e=0$ shares ($n=20000$, `d0` knob) showed good *parameter* recovery but a likelihood-value diagnostic that failed: LR $=2(-\ln L_{\text{truth}}-(-\ln L_{\hat\theta}))$ was $4.2/29/149$ at $e=0$ shares of $69\%/27\%/6\%$ (should be $\approx\chi^2_5$, median 4.35). Quadrature was ruled out ($-\ln L$ at the truth identical to 4 decimals for $K=150,\ldots,2400$). Diagnosis: the DGP drew true materials $M$ as an independent lognormal, a fourth latent the likelihood (3 latents, 2 observables) does not model; in the model $M$ is a deterministic function of $\omega$ (and $K,L$, prices). With `mdgp=fn_omega` ($\ln M=3+(\omega-\mu_\omega)/(1-\beta)$): LR $=2.3/3.3/5.2$ at shares $65\%/19\%/1.5\%$, $\hat\lambda=0.0204/0.0201/0.0201$ (truth 0.02), $\hat\delta_1=1.12/1.02/1.00$, $\hat\sigma_\psi=0.619/0.612/0.613$ (truth 0.6, consistently $\sim2\%$ high, single draw each). So the likelihood, Jacobian and conditioning in `9999-msl-implementation.qmd` are consistent with the model; the earlier inflation was a DGP error, not a likelihood error. **Consequences:** (i) the interior-only bias sizes reported in item 7 were measured on the unfaithful DGP and must be redone on `fn_omega` before being cited; (ii) the likelihood requires that real materials carry no independent noise beyond $(\omega,K,L,\text{prices})$ — recorded in the note as a maintained assumption. Logs: `Code/Products/logs/1400-msl-fnomega-d0_{1,0,m1}-macbook.log`. Logistics slip: the first two follow-up runs were chained sequentially on the MacBook though independent (idle capacity); the queued duplicate step survived a partial kill and was killed by PID after verifying its command line. Rule going forward: launch independent configs concurrently.

**9. C++ MSL (Version A, parametric) written and validated against the R reference (2026-09-18).** New standalone `Code/C-estimator/msl_estimator.cpp` (+ `Makefile.msl`), $\varepsilon$-space, no include of the ELVIS $M$-space header; per-firm Gauss-Legendre (Newton-computed nodes), threaded over fixed firm chunks summed in chunk order (deterministic), NLopt Nelder-Mead with two passes. The R harness (`1400-msl-sim-harness.R export=...`) writes the simulated data and its per-firm log-likelihoods at two test points. **Validation:** per-firm $|\ell^{C++}-\ell^{R}|\le3\times10^{-7}$ (relative $\le10^{-4}$, largest where $\ell\approx0$) for the full and interior-only likelihoods at both points ($n=3000$). **Fit check** on the $n=20000$ faithful-DGP dataset (`d0=0`, seed 2): C++ $-\ln L=21117.7908$, $\hat\lambda=0.020061,\hat\delta=(-0.0013,1.0189,0.2748),\hat\sigma_\psi=0.6118$, identical to the R fit to 4 digits; 8.6s on 4 threads vs. 504s in R ($\approx58\times$). Next: real-data driver (CSV export from the ELVIS sample, per-industry $f_\varepsilon$/$f_\omega$ as tabulated logspline lookups with pooled fallback), then logspline $f_\psi$.

**10. MSL C++ made portable across Apple Silicon and Intel (2026-09-18).** The source already used only the standard library + NLopt (no Accelerate, no `-march=native`); only the Makefile hard-coded `/opt/homebrew`. `Makefile.msl` now selects the Homebrew prefix by `uname -m` (override with `BREW=`). NLopt installed on the MacBook via `/usr/local/bin/brew install nlopt` (my earlier `which brew` failed only because non-interactive SSH has no `/usr/local/bin` on PATH). Built and run on both machines: $-\ln L$ identical to 8 decimals (full: 3169.16005918; interior-only at the second test point: 5441.74390382).

**11. Does the $e^*=0$ branch matter? Interior-only vs. full likelihood on the faithful DGP (2026-09-18; the redo item 8 asked for).** $n=20000$, `mdgp=fn_omega`, seed 2, same datasets as the full-likelihood runs; interior-only = same likelihood without the $f_\varepsilon(-\mathcal V)f_\omega(\omega_0)[1-F_\psi]$ term. At $e=0$ share 18.5%: full $\hat\delta=(-0.001,1.019,0.275)$, $\hat\sigma_\psi=0.612$ (LR 3.3) vs. interior-only $\hat\delta=(-0.31,0.50,\mathbf{-0.16})$, $\hat\sigma_\psi=0.436$ (LR $\approx4{,}250$): $\hat\delta_2$ flips sign (U-shape becomes inverted-U) and $\sigma_\psi$ is $\sim27\%$ low. At share 1.5%: full $\hat\delta=(-1.025,1.003,0.264)$, $\hat\sigma_\psi=0.613$ (LR 5.2) vs. interior-only $(-1.023,0.849,0.136)$, $\hat\sigma_\psi=0.550$ (LR 75, rejected against $\chi^2_{5,.95}=11.07$): $\delta_2\sim45\%$ low, $\sigma_\psi\sim8\%$ low. $\lambda$ is little affected in either. Conclusion for supervisors: the branch costs one normal CDF per firm and omitting it biases the cost-curve shape and $\sigma_\psi$ even at a 1.5% corner share, and the likelihood-value check detects it. Caveats: single draw per configuration, simulated DGP; the real-data share at $e^*=0$ is unknown. Logs: `Code/Products/logs/1400-msl-fnomega-intonly-d0_{0,m1}-macbook.log`.

**12a. Theory-coefficient ($\beta=1$) control variate tested as an independent check on CV-$R$'s fitted $\hat\beta$ — decisive confirmation, nested inside CV-$R$'s own bounds at every tested $\Delta$ (2026-09-18).** User's own idea, prompted while reviewing the "no free lunch" question: is $-\tau_P[M_i+(1-q(e'))e']$ — i.e. $R_i(\Delta)-t1_i$, algebraically the revenue identity's own accounting split — a better, theory-motivated alternative to CV-$R$'s regression-fitted $\hat\beta=0.998838$? Note this is **not** the same object as the already-implemented Loss moment (`row9_mode=2`), which deliberately drops $M_i$; this new one keeps it, so it's a $\beta=1$-fixed control variate, not a redefinition of the target.

**New `row9_mode=3` ("theory") added to `compute_row9` in `grid_estimator.cpp`**: `(R_real - t1/pgdp) - target`, no `cv_beta`/`cv_mu_c` needed since the coefficient is exact, not fitted. Threaded through all 4 CLI dispatch sites (`revgrid_indep`, `revgrid_fixedtheta`, `dvecdiag`, `omegadiag`). Compiled clean.

**Smoke test at the anchor point (Δ=0, seeded from CV's own converged γ): SE=13.59 vs. CV's own SE=13.53 at the same point — a 0.4% difference — and $\hat L_n=0.00015515$, matching CV's own $\hat L_n=0.00015515$ to 5 significant figures with $\gamma$ barely moved from the CV seed.** Immediate strong signal the two moments are near-identical in practice, exactly as predicted from $\hat\beta\approx0.9988$ being so close to 1.

**Grid design, reprioritized mid-run per user's request** to match CV-$R$'s own actual headline $\Delta$ set $\{-8,-6,-4,-2,0,+0.5,+1,+2\}\%$ rather than the original generic $\{-5,...,+5\}\%$ sweep — $-5\%$ (already run) kept as a stand-in for $-6\%$; the already-anchored $\Delta=0$ candidate reused rather than refit. Centers/SEs for the two new points not in the existing CV/Loss design file ($-8\%$, $+0.5\%$) computed exactly from fresh `revenue_baseline` forward-sims (not extrapolated): $-8\%$ center $=-719.597$, SE$=13.115$; $+0.5\%$ center $=-978.777$, SE$=15.006$. All cells same-point independently seeded from the anchor's converged $\gamma$ (no chaining), $n_{\text{burn}}=1000,n_{\text{keep}}=3000$, $\theta$ fixed at the standard operating point. (One process-management hiccup mid-run, no data impact: three overlapping wait/kill background tasks briefly raced each other while reprioritizing the $\Delta$ queue live — verified directly afterward that nothing was lost and no compute was wasted on the unwanted $-1\%$ batch before relaunching cleanly.)

**Result: at all 9 tested $\Delta$'s, a single clean, symmetric bracket — reject/pass/pass/reject/reject or reject/reject/pass/pass/reject shape, no exceptions.** Nothing resembling Loss's three-way pathology (empty region, vacuous region, erratic region). Converting each coarse passing interval back to real-revenue terms (+$\mu_c=2507.602596$, since the theory target is $E[R]-\mu_c$) and comparing directly against CV-$R$'s own final, finely-resolved hard-CI bounds:

| $\Delta$ | theory-implied CI (coarse, 5-pt) | CV-$R$ actual hard-CI | nested? |
|---:|---:|---:|:--:|
| $-8\%$ | [1788.0, 1827.4] | [1751, 1839] | yes |
| $-5\%$ | [1736.1, 1776.3] | [1727, 1801] | yes |
| $-4\%$ | [1727.5, 1768.2] | [1719, 1794] | yes |
| $-3\%$ | [1718.7, 1759.9] | [1707, 1786] | yes |
| $-2\%$ | [1709.1, 1750.9] | [1698, 1793] | yes |
| $0\%$ | [1700.5, 1744.6] | [1666, 1745] | yes |
| $+0.5\%$ | [1528.8, 1573.8] | [1492, 1575] | yes |
| $+1\%$ | [1356.1, 1402.5] | [1318, 1404] | yes |
| $+2\%$ | [1015.0, 1064.3] | [973, 1065] | yes |

**Every single one nests inside CV-$R$'s own bounds**, and several upper bounds match almost exactly ($0\%$: 1744.6 vs. 1745; $+0.5\%$: 1573.8 vs. 1575; $+1\%$: 1402.5 vs. 1404; $+2\%$: 1064.3 vs. 1065) — striking given this is only a coarse 5-point grid using a completely untuned coefficient, compared against CV-$R$'s own bisection-refined fine grid.

**Interpretation, decisive for the "no free lunch" discussion: CV-$R$'s fitted $\hat\beta=0.998838$ is not a data-mined number inflating precision — it's landing almost exactly on the value the revenue accounting identity hands you directly ($\beta=1$), and an entirely independent, non-data-fitted construction using that exact value reproduces CV-$R$'s own results closely, at every tested $\Delta$ including the critical $-8\%$ crossing point.** This directly answers the user's framing ("if they don't believe I can just give them the Loss [as an alternative validation]") — the theory-coefficient version is a far stronger independent check than Loss ever could be, precisely because Loss changes *what's measured* (introducing the zero-inflation pathology) while this changes only *which fixed coefficient* nets out $t1_i$ (none of Loss's problems, because $R_i-t1_i$ stays as smooth and non-degenerate as $R_i$ itself).

**Artifacts:** `Code/Products/1306-theory-anchor.csv`, `1306-theory-coarse-delta{0,1,2,3}.csv` ($-5\%$ through $-2\%$), `1306-theory-remaining-{delta0,deltam08,deltap005,deltap01,deltap02}.csv`. Scripts: `Code/Deconvolution/run-theory-grid.sh` (original, superseded mid-run), `run-theory-grid-remaining.sh` (the reprioritized follow-up). Not yet done: a fine (bisection-resolved) grid matching CV-$R$'s own precision — the coarse comparison above is already decisive for the intended purpose (validating $\hat\beta$), so a fine grid is not currently planned unless a tighter number is specifically wanted.

**12. First real-data MSL fit (2026-09-18; preliminary — Version A, normal $f_\omega$).** New `Code/Deconvolution/1401-msl-export.R` builds the input CSV from the ELVIS sample (`stage2_data`, lag_m, unincorporated, $\tau_P>0$, top 0.5% by $M^*$ trimmed): $n=28{,}892$ interior firms, 29 industries, identical to ELVIS's sample; corner ($\tau_P=0$) firms omitted (θ-free constants). $f_\varepsilon$: normal$(\mu_1,\sqrt{\text{var}})$ per industry from `1206`'s `eps_targets` incl. its `pooled_fallback` rule (89 firms). $f_\omega$ (first pass): normal, moment-deconvolved from $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ on ALL firms in the industry, pooled for the fallback industries — the tabulated logspline $f_\omega$ (fits exist for only 5 of 29 industries; the other 24 would need new `estimate_theta_W` runs) is the planned upgrade. C++ fit now optimizes $\log\lambda$ (real $\lambda\sim10^{-6}$–$10^{-5}$ next to $O(1)$ $\delta$'s). **Result** ($-\ln L=52{,}923.66$, 30-40s on 4 threads; three very different starts converge to the same optimum to 6 digits): $\hat\lambda=2.72\times10^{-5}$, $\hat\delta_0=7.42$, $\hat\delta_1=3.24$, $\hat\delta_2=0.233$ ($\hat\omega^*=\delta_1/2\delta_2=6.95$), $\hat\sigma_\psi=1.69$. $\delta_1,\delta_2>0$ (U-shape sign holds). **Contrast with ELVIS:** ELVIS $\hat\lambda=5.4\times10^{-7}$ (soft CI $[2.3,5.4]\times10^{-7}$), $\hat\omega^*\approx4.0$; MSL $\hat\lambda$ is $\sim50\times$ larger and $\hat\omega^*$ higher. At $\hat\lambda_{\text{MSL}}$ the FOC ceiling ($2\lambda M^*>1$) binds for 9,285/28,892 firms (32%) — a different regime from ELVIS's (where it binds for none) — so MSL's $\lambda$ is identified largely from how the ceiling truncates large firms' evasion. **Not yet done / caveats:** no standard errors or $\lambda$-profile; normal $f_\omega$ and $f_\varepsilon$ are misspecification risks; Version B ($f_\eta$, lag) not run; sensitivity to the 0.5% trim (ELVIS needed it for extreme evaders near the ceiling; the MSL likelihood handles the ceiling in the density, so it may not) not checked. Input: `Code/Products/msl/1401-msl-input-lag_m-trim0.005.csv`.

**13. Scale-coherence check for the planned model-faithful DGP (2026-09-18; `/tmp` script, not saved — rerun from the design below if needed).** Real-data ballparks (`stage2_data`, lag_m): 11 years (81-91), 5,968 firms, median 7 obs/firm, 20% corp, 10.3% of non-corp with $\tau_P=0$; interior $M^*$ median 8.5k (p10 1.4k, p90 76k); $\tau_P$ median 0.088 (p10 0.01, p90 0.13); $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ median 2.94, IQR-implied sd $\approx0.86$; $\mathcal V$ median 0.12, mean 0.056 $\Rightarrow$ $E[\ln(1+e/M)]\approx0.056$ (evasion $\approx$ 5-6% of true materials on average, since $E\varepsilon=0$); corp $\text{sd}(\varepsilon)=0.416$; OLS AR(1) of $\tilde{\mathcal W}$: 0.97. **Check:** simulate $\omega\sim N(2.94,0.85^2)$, $\ln M$ from the materials FOC (median $M\approx8{,}000$), $\tau_P\sim N(0.088,0.04^2)$ truncated, $\psi\sim N(0,\sigma_\psi^2)$ independent of $\omega$, $e^*=\max(0,[1-\exp(C(\omega)+\psi)/\tau_P]/2\lambda)$. At ELVIS-like $\lambda=5.4\times10^{-7}$ with ELVIS $\delta=(3.46,4.3,0.54)$ the model generates median $e/M\approx70$ and $E\ln(1+e/M)\approx3.7$ for EVERY $\sigma_\psi\in[10^{-3},1.7]$ — two orders of magnitude off the observed 0.056 (mechanism: $e=(1-r)/2\lambda\sim10^5$-$10^6$ unless $r=\exp(C+\psi)/\tau_P$ is within $\sim10^{-3}$ of 1, which an independent $\psi$ cannot deliver because the threshold $\psi_0(\omega)=\ln\tau_P-C(\omega)$ itself varies by $O(1)$ across $\omega$). At the MSL fit ($\lambda=2.7\times10^{-5}$, $\delta=(7.42,3.24,0.233)$, $\sigma_\psi=1.69$): $E\ln(1+e/M)=0.038$ (near 0.056), median $e/M=0.17$ among evaders, 87% of firms at $e^*=0$, 1.5% with $e>M$. **Reading (to be verified by the ELVIS-on-simulated-data test, not yet a conclusion about ELVIS):** with $\psi\perp\omega$ the model's implied $e/M$ scale pins $\lambda\gtrsim1/(2M)\sim10^{-5}$-$10^{-4}$; ELVIS's $\hat\lambda\sim5\times10^{-7}$ is only reachable through tilted conditional distributions that no independent-$\psi$ DGP generates, so a DGP-based test of both estimators can only be run in the MSL-like regime. Design agreed to build next (M-only first, then M+K/L): see chat 2026-09-18.

**14. Model-faithful DGP built (2026-09-18, `Code/Deconvolution/1410-dgp-model.R`; supersedes the ad-hoc `mdgp` in `1400`, which stays for the MSL-only recovery checks).** Adapted from GNR's CD Monte Carlo (`/Volumes/SSD Hans/Github/gnr/.../Table_1/Monte_Carlo_Code/CD`: AR(1) $\omega$ with $\eta$, Cobb-Douglas, materials from the static FOC, capital a state; their dynamic capital program is NOT reused — $K$ here is a reduced-form policy $k_t=\kappa_0+\kappa_1k_{t-1}+\kappa_2\omega_{t-1}+\nu_t$, predetermined at $t-1$; GNR has no evasion, so the tax/evasion block, corps and exempt sectors are added). All endogenous variables are functions of the shocks $(\eta,\varepsilon,\psi,\nu,\tau_P,\text{corp},\omega_0,k_0)$: $\omega_t=g_0+g_1\omega_{t-1}+\eta_t$; $x^{1-\beta}=\beta P\rho^{-\beta}e^{a_Kk+\omega}$; $e=\max(0,[1-e^{C(\omega)+\psi}/\tau_P]/2\lambda)$; $x^*=x+e$; $\mathcal V=\ln(\rho x^*/PY)-\ln\beta$; $\tilde{\mathcal W}=y-\beta(m^*-\mathcal V)-a_Kk$. M-only variant = `aK=0`. Parameters: $\beta=0.55,a_K=0.2,g_1=0.9$ (real OLS 0.97, GNR 0.8), stationary $\omega\sim(2.94,0.85^2)$, $\text{sd}(\varepsilon)=0.42$, $\delta_1=3.5,\delta_2=0.5$ ($\omega^*=3.5$ inside $\omega$'s range), $N=3000$, $T=11$ (balanced), 20% corps, 10% exempt sectors. **Checks (all pass):** FOC residual $4\times10^{-17}$, $\mathcal V$ and $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ identities $\sim10^{-15}$, corp $\mathcal V+\varepsilon=0$, $E[e^\varepsilon]=1.001$, AR(1) $\hat\gamma_1=0.9007$, corr$(\eta_t,k_t)=0.002$ (K predetermined). **Calibration** of $(\delta_0,\sigma_\psi)$ to real mean $E\ln(1+e/M)=0.056$ and a corner share $e^*=0$: feasible sets (many cells have no solution — the real mean evasion forces a high corner share at small $\lambda$): $\lambda=3\times10^{-5}$: share0 .85 → $(4.34,0.74)$; $1\times10^{-4}$: .70 → $(3.84,0.66)$, .85 → $(5.51,2.01)$; $3\times10^{-4}$: .50 → $(3.34,0.67)$, .70 → $(4.25,1.66)$; $10^{-3}$: .50 → $(3.28,1.90)$. **Baseline: $\lambda=10^{-4}$, $\delta_0=3.837$, $\sigma_\psi=0.660$, share0 = 0.70**, saved `Code/Products/msl/1410-sim-base-lam1e-4-s070.rds` (33,000 obs; 24,211 non-corp $\tau_P>0$). Vs. real: $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ median 2.918 (2.936), IQR 2.35-3.49 (2.35-3.51), $\mathcal V$ median 0.131 (0.119), IQR $-0.16$/0.43 ($-0.15$/0.34), $M^*$ median 9.0k (8.5k), $k$ 8.5/1.52 (8.5/1.5), $\tau_P$ median 0.098 (0.088). **Known mismatches:** $M^*$ tails too heavy (p99 2.0M vs 485k; sd$\ln x\approx2.0$ vs $\approx1.5$ — the real top 0.5% is trimmed anyway), $\mathcal V$ right tail heavier / left tail lighter than real, ceiling ($2\lambda M^*>1$) binds for 60% of firms, no labor/entry-exit/$\tau_S$ wedge. **Version A f_omega:** exact for the M-only DGP (unconditional marginal of $\omega$ is the true $N(2.94,0.85^2)$); misspecified when $K$ enters (ω_t depends on the state $k_t$), which is the intended A-vs-B comparison. **Next:** run MSL (A on M-only, A/B on M+K) and ELVIS (moment set A) on this panel against known truth.

## TOLLGATE: CV-R robustness investigation closed out, slides finalized (2026-09-18)

**Two comparison plots built and accepted as the visual evidence for the theory-coefficient check** (`Code/Deconvolution/1307-theory-vs-cv-plot.R` → `Paper/images/1307-theory-vs-cv.png`; `1308-theory-raw-plot.R` → `1308-theory-raw.png`). `1307`: both systems' individual tested candidates (pass/reject) plotted on the same real-revenue $y$-axis, at their actual $\Delta$'s — shows the theory-coefficient's passing points sitting in essentially the same range as CV-$R$'s at every tested $\Delta$. `1308`: the theory moment in its own native units ($R_i-t1_i$, no $+\mu_c$ shift), $y$-axis oriented with 0 (the $\Delta\to-1$ "no credit paid" limit) at top — confirms the expected shape directly: flat and closest to zero across $-8\%$ to $-2\%$, a sharp kink at $\Delta=0$, then a steep drop through $+0.5\%,+1\%,+2\%$. **Decision: present both as-is; refine bin/grid precision later only if asked** — the coarse 5-point grid is already sufficient to make the intended point (independent validation of $\hat\beta$), a finer bisection-matched grid is not planned unless specifically requested. Known cosmetic fix still owed before either goes in a real slide: `1308`'s legend currently overlaps the $y=0$ gridline/label.

**Full arc of this session's CV-$R$ robustness investigation, for anyone picking this up cold:**
1. Nail asked whether CV-$R$'s tightness vs. raw $R$'s vacuousness could be a numerical-truncation artifact, since efficient GMM weighting shouldn't care about a moment's raw variability in principle.
2. Built `omegadiag` (eigen-spectrum + loadings diagnostic) — found truncation structure is essentially identical between raw and CV-$R$ (same 4/10 kept, same 6 discarded); $R$'s own row is never truncated in either, only its rank differs (raw: largest eigenvalue; CV: mid-pack), tracking $1/\mathrm{Var}(R)$ almost exactly — textbook efficient weighting, not an artifact.
3. Reconstructed the full $\hat\Omega$/$\hat\gamma$ — found a real, measured side effect: `eps*e`'s own SE inflates $5.4\times$ under the CV fit, a genuine (if partial) "no free lunch" cost.
4. User asked the deeper question directly: if not a numerical artifact, what *is* the cost of the variance reduction? Answered with the `eps*e` spillover and the "higher power also catches unrelated misspecification" point; the "thin residual variance" candidate cost was correctly rejected by the user as not a real cost (it's the signal, not something given up).
5. Cross-check against the already-implemented Loss moment: found it fails in three distinct, diagnosable ways (empty region at $\Delta\le-2\%$ from zero-inflation, vacuous region at $-1\%$ to $+2\%$, erratic/sign-flipping region at $\ge+3\%$) — evidence that variance reduction alone doesn't guarantee a usable test; it has to preserve distributional regularity, which Loss's zero-inflated construction doesn't.
6. User's own proposed alternative — $R_i(\Delta)-t1_i$, a control variate with $\beta$ fixed at exactly 1 by the accounting identity rather than regression-fitted — tested decisively: clean, well-behaved brackets at all 9 target $\Delta$'s, nested inside CV-$R$'s own bounds everywhere, several bounds matching almost exactly. This is the strongest available evidence that $\hat\beta=0.998838$ isn't data-mined.

**Net effect on the "no free lunch" framing (CLAUDE.md's Counterfactual section already reflects this): two real costs remain** (the measured `eps*e` spillover; higher power also catching unrelated misspecification), **one candidate cost was dropped as not real** (thin residual), and **the exogeneity/data-mining concern is now substantially addressed** (not fully closed — the theory-coefficient check validates $\beta$, it doesn't independently verify every other maintained assumption) rather than fully open.

**Slides: draft outline agreed, not yet written to file as of this entry** — going into `Quarto-Slides/sections/660-cv-diagnostic.qmd` as an appendix section, included in the main deck build. Content: the truncation-not-the-cause finding, the $\hat\Omega$/$\hat\gamma$ comparison, the Loss-vs-theory-coefficient contrast, the two plots above, the revised "no free lunch" costs, and planned robustness (instrument comparison, lag_m vs. lag_2_cal_W).

**15. MSL on the model-faithful DGP: a quadrature artifact found and fixed, Version B derived, Monte Carlo results (2026-09-18).** (i) **DGP bug caught by its own identity checks:** `V` was computed as $\ln(\rho x^*/PY)-\ln\beta$; $x$ is nominal spending so the extra $\rho$ was wrong (identities failed for $\rho\ne1$, 3.1 and 1.7). Fixed in `1410-dgp-model.R`; the baseline ($\rho=1$) was unaffected. (ii) **Symptom:** in the M-only MC, 5 of 10 seeds (and 3 of 10 in M+K) ran to a degenerate solution ($\lambda\sim0.2$–1, $\delta_0\approx-12$, $\delta_{1,2}\approx0$, i.e. "no evasion") whose $-\ln L$ was ~9,000 BELOW the value at the true parameters. Diagnosed: at the truth the density integrates to 1.0000 over $(\mathcal V,\tilde{\mathcal W})$ for every $\tau_P$, but at the degenerate point it integrated to 2.52 — not a density. **Cause:** with a tight ceiling the integrand in $\varepsilon$ has a spike of relative width $\sim10^{-5}$ at the end of the window where $1-2\lambda e\to0$; fixed-node Gauss-Legendre over the whole $\varepsilon$ window mis-integrates it, and the optimizer exploited the error. **Fix (`msl_estimator.cpp`, `quad=1`, default; `quad=0` keeps the legacy rule):** integrate over $s=\ln(1-2\lambda e)$: $|d\varepsilon|=\text{den}\,ds/(2\lambda(M^*-e))$ and $J=2\lambda M^*/\text{den}$, so the interior term is $\int f_\psi(\psi(s))\,\frac{M^*}{M^*-e}\,f_\omega f_\varepsilon\,ds$, $\psi=\ln\tau_P+s-C(\omega)$ (Gaussian in $s$, no spike); window from where $f_\psi$ is non-negligible. New rule normalizes to 1.0000 at the truth, at the degenerate point, and at two other extreme parameter sets (legacy: 2.52 at the degenerate point); identical to legacy to 8 decimals at the validated test points; **the real-data fit is unchanged to 6 digits** ($\lambda=2.724\times10^{-5}$, $-\ln L=52923.658$) — the artifact lived only in degenerate regions. (iii) **Monte Carlo (10 seeds, $N=3000\times T=11$, baseline $\lambda=10^{-4},\delta=(3.837,3.5,0.5),\sigma_\psi=0.66$, 3 diffuse starts/seed, `1412-msl-mc.R`), new quadrature: every seed converges to a single mode.** M-only, Version A (exact there): $\lambda$ +2.4% (t 0.9), $\delta_0$ +5.6%, $\delta_1$ +3.9%, $\delta_2$ +4.7%, $\sigma_\psi$ +3.3% (all $|t|\le1.3$). M+K, Version A: $\lambda$ −4.9% (t −3.2), $\delta_0/\delta_1/\delta_2$ +38/+39/+62% ($|t|$ 6-10), $\sigma_\psi$ −1.8%; LR vs. truth ~40-60 (detectable). M+K, **Version B**: $\lambda$ +1.8% (t 1.6), $\delta_0/\delta_1/\delta_2$ +12.7/+11.2/+16.3% ($t\approx2.7$-3.1), $\sigma_\psi$ +2.1%. M-only, Version B: +3.8/+7.2/+5.0/+5.7/+3.3% (all $|t|\le1.5$). (iv) **Version B, derived, and a correction to the earlier dynamic-likelihood note.** `9999-density-trans.qmd` Step 3 (and `610-MSL.qmd`'s "Likelihood contribution of a firm" slide, `eq-Lunc-dynamic`) integrates $\varepsilon_{t-1}$ against the prior $f_\varepsilon$, treating it as independent of everything else; but $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}=\omega_{t-1}+(1-\beta)\varepsilon_{t-1}$ is observed, so $\varepsilon_{t-1}$ is NOT independent of the conditioning variable — the integrand also needs the marginal $f_\omega(\omega_{t-1})$ (equivalently, use the posterior of $\omega_{t-1}$ given $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}$). The joint density of $(\mathcal V_t,\tilde{\mathcal W}_t,\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1})$ differs from the conditional by $f_{\tilde{\mathcal W}}(\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1})$, a $\theta$-free constant, so either gives the same $\hat\theta$. **With normal $f_\varepsilon,f_\omega,f_\eta$ it is closed form:** $\omega_t\mid\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}\sim N\big(\gamma_0+\gamma_1m_1,\ \sigma_\eta^2+\gamma_1^2v_1\big)$, $m_1=\mu_\omega+\frac{\sigma_\omega^2}{\sigma_\omega^2+b^2\sigma_\varepsilon^2}(\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}-\mu_\omega-b\mu_\varepsilon)$, $v_1=\sigma_\omega^2-\frac{\sigma_\omega^4}{\sigma_\omega^2+b^2\sigma_\varepsilon^2}$, $b=1-\beta$, $\sigma_\eta^2=\sigma_\omega^2(1-\gamma_1^2)$ — so Version B needs NO C++ change, only firm-specific conditional $(\mu_\omega,\sigma_\omega)$ columns; AR(1) by moments ($\gamma_1=\text{Cov}(\tilde{\mathcal W}_t,\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1})/\text{Var}(\omega)$). Why Version A fails with $K$: the likelihood uses $M^*$ (which contains $k$) while $f_\omega$ is unconditional, but $\omega_t$ is correlated with $k_t$ (through $\omega_{t-1}$). Residual +11-16% in Version B is plausibly the un-used information in $k_t$ about $\omega_{t-1}$ beyond $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}$ (not tested). **Not yet fixed: `9999-density-trans.qmd` Step 3 and the `610-MSL.qmd` slide still carry the prior-based $\varepsilon_{t-1}$ integral — needs user's OK before editing slides.** Outputs: `Code/Products/msl/1412-msl-mc-*.csv`.

**16. Sizing the derivation flaw: old (prior-based) vs. corrected (posterior) dynamic likelihood (2026-09-18).** Same 10 M+K panels (`1412-msl-mc.R fomega=A|Bprior|B`): Version A (no conditioning): $\delta_{0,1,2}$ +38/+39/+62%, $\lambda$ −5%; **old derivation** ($\varepsilon_{t-1}$ integrated against its PRIOR: $\omega_t\mid\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}\sim N(\gamma_0+\gamma_1(\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}-b\mu_\varepsilon),\ \sigma_\eta^2+\gamma_1^2b^2\sigma_\varepsilon^2)$): +19.9/+18.8/+28.4% ($t$ 4.2/4.9/5.6), $\lambda$ +1.4%; **corrected** (posterior): +12.7/+11.2/+16.3% ($t$ 2.7/2.8/3.1), $\lambda$ +1.8%. Conditioning on the lag removes about half the bias; prior$\to$posterior removes about 40% of the remainder. So the flaw in Step 3 of `9999-density-trans.qmd` / `610-MSL.qmd`'s `eq-Lunc-dynamic` is real and empirically material (though modest in this calibration, where $b\sigma_\varepsilon=0.19$ is small next to $\sigma_\omega=0.85$; it grows with measurement noise). **Implication for the real-data MSL fit (item 12), which was Version A:** with $K$ in the model, Version A inflates $\hat\delta_1,\hat\delta_2$ by 40-60% in simulation, so the reported real-data $(\hat\delta_1,\hat\delta_2)=(3.24,0.233)$ is probably too high; rerun with Version B before use. Residual +11-16% in Version B not yet explained (candidate: information in $k_t$ about $\omega_{t-1}$ beyond $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}$). CLAUDE.md's MSL entry corrected; notes and slides pending the user's confirmation of the drafted text.

**17. Version B without normality (2026-09-19; user's question: the derivation assumes no functional forms — can we still do it without the closed form?).** Yes. $h_i(\omega)=\int f_\eta(\omega-g(\omega'))\,p_i(\omega')\,d\omega'$ with $p_i\propto f_\omega(\omega')f_\varepsilon((\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-1}-\omega')/b)$ is a per-firm 1-D integral for any densities, and it does not depend on $\theta$ (only stage-1 objects and $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-1}$), so it is tabulated once per firm on a $\theta$-free $\omega$-grid ($[\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}-b\varepsilon_{\max},\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it}-b\varepsilon_{\min}]$) and interpolated in every likelihood evaluation; the $\theta$-loop is unchanged. Checked in R with black-box densities against the Gaussian closed form: max relative error $2.7\times10^{-15}$. Cost $\sim10^9$ evaluations once for 28,892 firms. The closed form used in the simulation (item 15) was only a shortcut for the Gaussian DGP. Real data: corp $\varepsilon$ is skewed (third central moments 0.087, 0.247, 0.012, −0.075 with variances 0.12, 0.24, 0.13, 0.24 in the first four `eps_targets` rows: skewness $\approx$ 2.1, 2.1, 0.25, −0.64), so the normal $f_\varepsilon$ in the first real-data MSL export (item 12) was misspecified; nonparametric $f_\varepsilon$ (empirical/kernel from corps, `np_pdf`) and logspline $f_\omega$ (`estimate_theta_W`, exists for 5 of 29 industries), $f_\eta$ (Fourier/logspline deconvolution) are needed. Proposed next steps, not started: (a) C++ extension reading per-industry tabulated $f_\varepsilon,f_\omega,f_\eta$ + $(\gamma_0,\gamma_1)$ and building $h_i$ on load; (b) a NON-normal DGP (skewed $\varepsilon$, non-Gaussian $\eta$) to show normal-Version-B is biased while the tabulated version is not.
