## 2026-09-19 — MSL: Version B normalization check; where the residual Version-B bias comes from (OPEN ITEM 8)

**Normalization (`Code/Deconvolution/1421-msl-normcheck.R`).** Full density (interior + $e^*=0$ branch), integrated over $(\mathcal V,\tilde{\mathcal W}_t)$ on a 2-D grid, $s=\ln(1-2\lambda e)$ rule, Version A vs. B (per-firm conditional $f_{\omega_t|\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}}$), 45 configs ($\theta\in$ {truth, off-truth, $\lambda=10^{-6}$, $10^{-3}$, degenerate $\lambda=0.5,\delta_0=-12$} × $M\in\{20,150,2000\}$ × $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}\in\{1.5,2.94,4.5\}$). **1.0000 in 43/45**, A and B alike; the 2 exceptions ($\lambda=10^{-6}$, $M=20$: 0.98) are the $\mathcal V$-grid edge ($\ln(1+e/M)\approx10$). **The check must hold the TRUE $M$ fixed** ($M^*=Me^{\mathcal V+\varepsilon}$, $d\varepsilon\cdot J=ds$ exactly), the conditioning the Jacobian was derived under; fixing $M^*$ instead gives 0.75–1.56 (misleading, not a bug). The C++ rule uses $M^*$ as data, factor $M^*/(M^*-e)$; the fixed-$M$ analog has factor 1 — both are the same density. Legacy $\varepsilon$-rule gives 1.56 at the degenerate point; $s$-rule 1.0000. NOT shown: normalization conditional on observed $M^*$ (not expected to hold — $M^*$ shares the latents).

**Item 8, 10-seed withK MC (oracle Gaussian conditionals from the TRUE $\omega$; `1412-msl-mc.R fomega=Bo|C`).** `Bo` (info = $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}$ only, projection with true $\omega$) reproduces Version B exactly ($\delta_{0,1,2}$ +12.8/+11.3/+16.5%, $t\approx2.6$–3.1) ⇒ the residual bias is NOT moment-estimation error in $\gamma_1,\sigma_\omega$. `C` (info = $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1},k_{t-1},k_t$) cuts it to +4.8/+3.1/+3.4% ($|t|\le1.1$, not significant); $\lambda$ +3.2% ($t=2.9$, up from +1.8%, $t=1.6$), $\sigma_\psi$ +2.7%. Note $R^2$ of the projection barely moves (0.772→0.774, resid sd 0.406→0.403) yet the bias falls 3–5×: the $k$ information matters far more for the likelihood than its variance share suggests (mechanism not yet pinned down — likely the $M^*$–$k$ link). **Caveat: `C` is an oracle (true $\omega$ in the projection, $k$-law-of-motion implicitly known); a feasible version must estimate this conditional without observing $\omega$ (e.g. from the model's own $k_t=\kappa(k_{t-1},\omega_{t-1},\nu_t)$ structure).** Outputs `Code/Products/msl/1412-msl-mc-withK-{Bo,C}.csv`.

**2026-09-19 (later) — the catch in the density derivation, exact-observables equivalence, feasible Cf.** (i) **Where the derivation went wrong:** the AR(1) step used "$\omega_{t-1}$ is sufficient (Markov)" and then replaced $\omega_{t-1}$ by $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1}$. Sufficiency holds for the TRUE state, not for a noisy proxy: once $\omega_{t-1}$ is integrated out, its posterior must condition on the WHOLE observed information set — $k_{t-1},k_t$ (in the DGP $k_t=\kappa_0+\kappa_1k_{t-1}+\kappa_2\omega_{t-1}+\nu_t$ is a second signal on $\omega_{t-1}$), and in principle $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-2},\dots$, lagged $M^*$, and $L$ in real data (not tested). The K,L point and the lag-$\omega$ point are one issue. (ii) **Exact-observables likelihood** (density of $(\mathcal V,M^*)$, $\omega$-integral, $|J|=|h'(e)|=2\lambda/(1-2\lambda e)$; $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ is redundant given $(\mathcal V,M^*,k)$ via $\tilde{\mathcal W}=(1-\beta)(\ln M^*-\mathcal V)-\ln\beta-a_Kk$) equals the current $\varepsilon$-space likelihood times the $\theta$-free factor $(1-\beta)/M^*$ — verified to $10^{-9}$ (`1423-msl-exact-obs-check.R`). So structure/Jacobian/corner branch are right; only $f_\omega(\omega_t\mid I)$ was under-conditioned (an earlier "off the manifold" worry was wrong). (iii) **Score at truth** (`1422-msl-score-check.R`, N=20,000, 9 seeds): B has a same-signed nonzero score (pooled z about +3/−4/+5 on $\delta_{0,1,2}$); oracle C and feasible Cf scatter around 0 (mean $\delta$ step about +2%, mixed signs). (iv) **Feasible Cf** (`fomega=Cf`): $\varepsilon_t\perp(\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1},k_{t-1},k_t)$, so regressing $\tilde{\mathcal W}_t$ on them gives $\omega_t$'s projection coefficients, $\text{Var}(\omega|X)=$ resid var $-(1-\beta)^2\sigma_\varepsilon^2$; no true $\omega$. **10-seed MC (withK): $\delta_{0,1,2}$ +5.2/+3.5/+3.9% ($|t|\le1.2$), $\lambda$ +3.2% ($t=2.8$), $\sigma_\psi$ +2.7% — same as oracle C.** **Decision (user):** this is the best available; document as a known limitation — residual small upward bias in $\hat\delta$ (a few %) and $\hat\lambda$ (about 3%), from conditioning on an incomplete information set (earlier $\tilde{\mathcal W}$ lags, $M^*$ lags not used); exact only under joint normality (non-normal densities need the filter integral); k-signal relies on the predetermined-$k$ timing.

## 2026-09-19 (late) — Data-calibrated single-industry DGP (industry 321) and what it showed

**Decisions (user):** model and assumptions untouched, incl. ONE common $\varepsilon$ distribution for corps and unincorporated (corp ecdf); calibrate the DGP to OBSERVABLES of one industry (321: 529 corps, 2,229 interior firms, mean $\mathcal V$ 0.147), not to unobserved features ($e$'s share/skew are results, not targets). Deconvolved $f_u$ (P-spline; `030-np-deconv-funs.R`, `291-bs-deconv.R`, saved `bs_mle_data.RData`; NP-$\varepsilon$ = kernel of corp $\varepsilon$): 321 mean 0.127, sd 0.044, skew 0.57, $P(u<0.05)$=0.7% (normal-$\varepsilon$ version: 0.113/0.054/1.28) — essentially no mass at zero. Note: fitted $f_u\otimes f_\varepsilon$ reproduces $\mathcal V$'s mean/skew but only 0.372 of its 0.414 sd; moment-decomposed $u$ (sd 0.185, skew −4) is inconsistent with $u\ge0$ — dispersion of $u$ is not pinned down by the fit.

**DGP (`1450-dgp-321.R`, `1451-calibrate-321.R`):** $\beta=0.387,\alpha_K=0.322,\alpha_L=0.284$; corp share 18.8%, exempt 2.3% of non-corps; $\varepsilon$ = bootstrap of corp ecdf (mean 0, sd 0.370, skew 1.68); $\tau_P$ empirical resampling; $(\omega,k,l)$ stationary law + $k_t,l_t$ laws of motion ESTIMATED SEPARATELY for corps and non-corps (errors-in-variables via $\text{var}(\omega)=\text{var}(W)-(1-\beta)^2\text{var}(\varepsilon)$, $\text{cov}(\omega,k)=\text{cov}(W,k)$); $\gamma_1=0.97$ (moment formula gave 1.07); materials FOC exact ($V=u-\varepsilon$, $\tilde W=\omega+(1-\beta)\varepsilon$ identities verified; a price-constant bug that shifted $V$ was found and fixed: $pc=\ln P-\beta\ln\rho$ must enter both the FOC and $y$). Evasion block fitted by SMM to $\mathcal V$'s marginal (7 quantiles + mean, bootstrap-SE weights): $\lambda=8.2\times10^{-4},\delta_0=2.54,\delta_1=3.41,\delta_2=0.04,\theta_\psi=0.53$ ($\psi=\theta-\text{Gamma}(1,\theta)$), obj 325 on 8 targets. Tails match (q5,q10,q90 within 0.5 se); the middle misses (q25/q50/q75 sim −0.085/0.145/0.348 vs −0.025/0.229/0.429) and q95 overshoots (0.676 vs 0.605); mean $\mathcal V$ 0.114 vs 0.147. Structural reason: $e$ does not scale with $M$ ($e\perp M\mid\omega$, $\text{sd}(\ln M\mid\omega)\approx1.4$ in 321), so a middle-firm 15% overreport implies small firms far above it. Implied (not targeted): 98% of interior firms evade, $E[e/M]=0.157$ but median 0.047, $e$ bunched near the ceiling (top 1% hold 1.1%). $\psi$'s shape irrelevant (obj 325–326 for gamma shape 0.3/1/3); $\delta_2\approx0$ (ω too narrow, sd 0.38) so several parameter sets fit equally. **Not targeted, by design:** cor$(V,\tilde W)$, cor$(V,\ln M^*)$ — the corps ($V=-\varepsilon$) already show cor$(V,\tilde W)=+0.39$ (model implies $-0.5$) and cor$(V,\ln M^*)=+0.31$, so evasion cannot drive them; cor$(V,\ln\tau_P)$ also mechanical ($\tau_P$ built from $M^*$). Saved `Code/Products/msl/1451-calib-321-psik1.rds`. Estimators NOT yet run on it. **Also this session:** naive MSM on the M+K sim (`1431-msm-sim.R`): rejects even at the TRUE $\theta$ (J 91,773 unweighted / 80,893 with $f_\omega$ importance weights, vs $\chi^2_{5,.95}=11.07$, test inversion = $d_g$ not $d_g-d_\theta$); ELVIS-on-sim cube was stopped (weak $\gamma=0$ seeding; also the 70% $e^*=0$ share of that DGP); **plan (user, 2026-09-19): support ELVIS from REAL data — evaluate naive-MSM statistic and MSL likelihood at ELVIS's best point; run ELVIS's test at MSL's and MSM's best points; simulations deferred as future robustness if slow (paper draft due within two weeks).**

## 2026-09-19 (night) — Real-data cross-test of the three estimators at each other's best points (lag_m, trim 0.5%)
Best points: ELVIS (cube, moment set A) $\lambda=5.427\times10^{-7},(\delta_0,\delta_1,\delta_2)=(3.464,4.3,0.54)$; MSL (Version A, normal densities) $\lambda=2.724\times10^{-5},\delta=(7.418,3.243,0.2335),\sigma_\psi=1.694$ ($-\ln L=52{,}923.66$); naive MSM (S=500, overID) $\lambda=6.85\times10^{-4},\delta=(-5.42,14.96,3.42)$, $J=29{,}455$. **ELVIS's test ($TS=2n\hat L$, $n=32{,}232$, $\chi^2_{9,.95}=16.92$; $\delta_0,\gamma$ free, seeded from ELVIS's converged fit, NM, `1440-elvis-at-*.csv`):** ELVIS point $\hat L=2.15\times10^{-4}$, TS 13.9 (not rejected; reproduces the cube exactly); MSL point $\hat L=0.0266$, TS 1,715 (reject); MSM point $\hat L=0.0360$, TS 2,320 (reject). Caveat: $\hat L$ at the MSL/MSM points is a Nelder-Mead local search; pass1→pass2 moved little (0.0287→0.0266; MSM unchanged); a better optimum could only lower TS, gap is ~100×. **Naive MSM statistic at fixed $\theta$ (`1442-msm-eval-real.R`, S=250, test inversion, $\chi^2_{5,.95}=11.07$):** ELVIS point 66,229; MSL point 94,979; MSM's own best 29,326 — rejects everywhere, incl. its own minimum (as on the sim, where it rejects even the TRUE $\theta$). **MSL, two tests of $H_0:\theta=\theta_0$ ($\sigma_\psi$ profiled), both require a correct likelihood (no goodness-of-fit component):** LR vs its max (`1441-msl-lr-at-points.R`, $\chi^2_{4,.95}=9.49$): ELVIS point 46,481 (profiled $\sigma_\psi\approx30$–50), MSM point 23,433 — reject; plant-clustered efficient-score test (`1443-msl-score-test.R`, 4,954 plants): MSL max $T=0.0$ (sanity), ELVIS point 3,392 (OPG 28,852), MSM point 2,701 — reject. So MSL's likelihood rejects ELVIS's point; this shows the two disagree, not which is right (Version A is biased on the sim: +38–62% in the $\delta$'s, normal $f_\varepsilon$ vs skewed real $\varepsilon$). Information-matrix test considered and dropped (user: low power, any outcome uninformative). Story for the draft: ELVIS's moments hold at its own point and fail at the others'; naive MSM rejects everywhere (no discriminating power); MSL and ELVIS disagree on $\theta$ and MSL has no specification check.

**TOLLGATE 2026-09-19 (night): keep ELVIS as the headline; MSL is a parametric robustness check to refine later.** User's argument, adopted: MSL is parametric (normal $f_\psi$, $f_\varepsilon$, $f_\omega$; Version A unconditional $f_\omega$) and may impose shapes that are not true; the distribution of the unobservables is left free in ELVIS (only $\hat\beta$ from stage 1 is used). So MSL's rejection of ELVIS's point (LR 46,481; plant-clustered score $T=3{,}392$) says the best point under MSL's specification is elsewhere, not that ELVIS is wrong; ELVIS is robust in that sense. Together with ELVIS not rejecting its own point ($TS=13.9$) while rejecting the MSL and MSM points (1,715 / 2,320), and naive MSM rejecting everywhere (incl. the true $\theta$ on the sim), the story for the draft is: keep ELVIS; report MSL/naive MSM as comparisons. **Honest limits to keep in the paper:** this argues ELVIS is robust to distributional assumptions; it does NOT show ELVIS recovers the truth. Simulation validation of ELVIS (data-calibrated 321 DGP; earlier ELVIS cube on the M+K sim was stopped: $\gamma=0$ seeding, 70% $e^*=0$ share) is deferred as future robustness. Future MSL refinement: feasible Gaussian/general $f_{\omega_t\mid\tilde{\mathcal W}_{t-1},k_{t-1},k_t}$ (Cf), skewed or nonparametric $f_\varepsilon$ (corp ecdf/kernel), logspline $f_\psi$. CLAUDE.md updated (MSL demoted to secondary, tollgate paragraph added, ELVIS marked headline).

Deck not re-rendered. Still open: old $\chi^2_{1,.95}=3.84$ df language in that file's "Results of the test" slide; drafted $\varepsilon_{t-1}$-posterior corrections to `610-MSL.qmd`/`9999-density-trans.qmd` awaiting user OK; nothing committed.

## 2026-09-21 — Naive MSM run as a grid search for Tim (two 7-point cubes, $\delta_0$ profiled); improved naive MSM (ω-weighted, clipping fixed) shelved

- **Request (Tim):** grid-search and test the naive MSM. `1445-msm-cube.R` (built on `1442`'s draws; center + 6 neighbours: $\lambda\times/\div2$, $\delta_1\pm1$, $\delta_2\pm0.2$; $\delta_0$ profiled 1-D at each point; same draws at every point; $J=n\hat L_n$ vs $\chi^2_{5,.95}=11.07$, lag_m, S=250, overID with the $\psi\ln M$ moment). Cube 1 centered on the MSM optimizer's point ($6.85\times10^{-4},-5.41,14.96,3.42$): **all 7 reject, J 29,306–29,482** (flat ridge, under 1% variation; center not the minimum). Cube 2 centered on the ELVIS point ($5.43\times10^{-7},4.3,0.54$): **all 7 reject, J 30,589–35,783** (center 32,940; higher $\delta_2$ lowers J, lower $\delta_2$ raises it). 14/14 reject by a factor ~2,700+, so the test favors neither point. Outputs: `Code/Products/1445-msm-cube{,-elvis}-lag_m.csv`, `Paper/images/1446-msm-cube{,-elvis}-lag_m-3d.png` (`1446-msm-cube-3dplot.R`), `Paper/tbls/1447-msm-cube-table.png` (`1447-msm-cube-table.R`). Slides: `610-MSL.qmd` — "Results of the test" (table), new "The two grid searches" (plots + bullets); "Testing with data" text corrected from exactly identified ($d_g=4$) to the overidentified case actually run ($d_g=5$); citation key is `@GourierouxMonfort1996`. Table slide height 45% still overflowed by 6pt; shrinking to 40% made the table illegible, so the table sits alone at 80% and the bullets moved to the plots slide (checked with a 2-pass `lualatex`; no overfull on either page).
- **What the naive MSM does NOT do (verified in `1442-msm-eval-real.R`):** moments are an equal-weight average over draws from the corporate $\varepsilon$ pool; $\omega_{hat}=\tilde{\mathcal W}-(1-\beta)\varepsilon$ is computed from each draw but $f_\omega$ (and $f_\psi$, and the Jacobian) never enter; the only restriction is the $e>0$ feasibility filter. **Also found: the upper FOC support $e<1/(2\lambda)$ is not enforced — draws above it are CLIPPED (`pmax(1-2*lambda*e, 1e-6)`, $\ln 10^{-6}\approx-13.8$).** At the MSM optimizer ($1/(2\lambda)\approx730$) this may hit many draws; at the ELVIS point ($\approx10^6$) it should hardly bind. Not measured — the share of clipped draws per cube point was offered and not run.
- **Improved naive MSM shelved (user's decision):** wanted weighting by the unconditional $f_\omega$ (deconvolved from $\tilde{\mathcal W}$) plus rejecting rather than clipping infeasible draws. The nonparametric $f_\omega$ machinery exists (`estimate_np_theta_omega` in `030-np-deconv-funs.R`, driven by `425_omega.R`) but the only saved fit (`Code/Products/omega_ar1_deconv_mle.RData`) covers 5 industries (331, 322, 369, 313, 321) on the Aug-2025 first stage ($\beta\approx0.29$), not the current 29-industry stage-2 data. Plan agreed but not built: deconvolve non-thin industries individually, pool the thin ones, on `stage2_data` (`tilde_cal_W`, `beta`, corporate `epsilon`; $f_\varepsilon$ by plain kernel density with a zero/decaying tail outside the observed range). User then dropped it as too much work for a test expected to fail. Left in `1445-msm-cube.R` as untested code with `wfix = FALSE` as default (reproduces the published cubes; `wfix=TRUE` = per-industry-normal $f_\omega$ weights, weight 0 for infeasible draws).
- **Conceptual note kept for Tim:** $\varepsilon\perp$ primitives $(u,\omega,\psi,M,K,L,\dots)$, but NOT independent of the observables $\mathcal V=u-\varepsilon$ and $\tilde{\mathcal W}=\omega+(1-\beta)\varepsilon$ built from it; drawing $\varepsilon$ from the pool while plugging in each firm's own $(\mathcal V,\tilde{\mathcal W})$ ignores $\varepsilon\mid(\mathcal V,\tilde{\mathcal W})$. The simulation result (naive test rejects even at the true $\theta$, `1431-msm-sim.R`) is the clean answer to "is this the model or the moment construction."

## 2026-09-21 — Two-tax-rate first stage, Phases 1–2: net-of-tax first stage barely moves $\hat\beta,\hat\alpha_L,\hat\varepsilon$; $\hat\alpha_K$ is unstable in both versions

- **Definitions settled with the user:** the share's denominator is NOMINAL GROSS OUTPUT (`10_data_wrangling.R:260`, `materials_share = nom_mats/nom_gross_output`; `pg`; consistent with $y=\ln$ gross output; comment "nominal gross output in GNR"; older scripts used `materials/sales`). Net sales = `nom_gross_output − t1`, net materials = `nom_mats − t2`, with $t_1=\tau_S\cdot$`nom_sales`, $t_2=\tau_P\cdot$`nom_mats` (the df carries the rates). An earlier version of the diagnostic used `nom_sales` in the denominator and mis-attributed a +0.03 $\hat\beta$ shift to netting taxes — that was the denominator change (median `nom_sales/nom_gross_output`=0.92); redone with the correct denominator and the "salesd" control dropped.
- **Phase 1 (`1470-net-tax-first-stage-diag.R`, `Code/Products/1470-*`):** gross vs. net first stage via `first_stage_panel_me` on a common sample (40,420 rows; 85 fewer than current: net$\le0$, NA $\tau_S$, share cutoffs). $\hat\beta$: n-weighted mean change $-0.006$ (median $-0.007$); 27/29 industries move $<0.02$; outliers 353 (2 corporations, $+0.07$) and 372 ($+0.023$). $\hat\varepsilon$: sd 0.398 → 0.413 (n-weighted), skewness essentially unchanged; corporate sd of the wedge median 0.041 vs. $\varepsilon$ sd ≈ 0.40; corr($\hat\varepsilon_{gross}$, wedge) ≈ +0.24 (industries with ≥50 corporations) — a mild omitted variable; median unincorporated $\mathcal V$ shifts +0.012.
- **Phase 2 (`1471-net-tax-pf-params.R`, `Code/Products/1471-net-pf-params.{RData,csv}`, `Paper/tbls/1471-net-pf-params-table.png`):** `estimate_prod_fn_bounds` re-run for both instruments (lag_m, lag_2_cal_W), 29 industries, gross vs. net on the same sample (116 fits, 0 failed, 3 non-converged: 351/lag_m net, 342/lag_m gross, 361/lag_2_cal_W net). $\hat\alpha_L$ and $\hat\beta$ barely move (mean $|\Delta|$ ≈ 0.010–0.016; industries with n≥300: 0.009–0.011). $\hat\alpha_K$ has large swings in a handful of industry-instrument cells that are **optimizer/identification instability, not a tax effect**: 369/lag_m 0.271→1.000 (upper bound), 322/lag_2_cal_W 0.880→0.183, 382/lag_2_cal_W 1.000→0.247 — the same cells sit at bounds or at implausible levels in the gross fit too (e.g. 321/lag_2_cal_W $\hat\alpha_K=0.82$–0.89, 331/lag_2_cal_W 0.03). Mean returns to scale 0.990 (gross) vs. 0.985 (net). Table covers the paper's five industries (331, 322, 369, 313, 321).
- **Not verified:** that the gross re-run on the common sample reproduces the stored `1100-MSL-opttax.RData` estimates exactly (should be near-identical apart from the 85 dropped rows). **Not done:** $f_u$ deconvolution, and any stage-2 rerun on the net objects (ELVIS moments, grids, counterfactual would all need re-running). Stage 1 itself (`931.1`, `1100-MSL-opttax.RData`) untouched; all net objects live in parallel `1470-*`/`1471-*` files. To bring to supervisors: the wedge is a real but numerically small omitted variable for $\hat\beta,\hat\alpha_L,\hat\varepsilon$; $\hat\alpha_K$'s instability is a separate, pre-existing issue worth flagging.

## 2026-09-21 (later) — Why my gross re-run differed from the paper's PF table: dropped rows + a fragile $\hat\alpha_K$ objective; paper now points at the trimmed results file

- **Which table/file is right (user):** the paper's PF table must use the TRIMMED results (`Code/Products/deconv_prod_fun_trim.RData`, built by `300-deconv-prod.R` after the 2026-07-27 change: first-stage trimming `log_mats_share > log(threshold_cut)` and the second instrument changed from the double lag of $\mathcal W-\alpha_Kk-\alpha_Ll$ to the double lag of $\mathcal W$ itself). `Paper/sections/250-pf.qmd` was still loading the OLD `deconv_prod_fun.RData` (lines 31 and 133) — **changed both to `deconv_prod_fun_trim.RData`**. Other scripts still load the old file (`425_omega.R`, `490-boot-pf-prod.R`, `Code/Colombia/_150-from-fortran-grn.R`) — not touched. Prose in `250-pf.qmd` says the five industries are "311, 313, 352, 351, 321 (top 5 by revenue)" but the table (`PF_me_tbl`) actually shows 331, 322, 369, 313, 321 — text is stale (not edited).
- **Instrument notation:** in this PF estimation `lag_2_cal_W` = `lag(cal_W, 2)` = $\mathcal W_{it-2}$ (NO tilde), matching the paper's column header; `CLAUDE.md`/memory write $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$ (with tilde) — inconsistent with the code as of the 2026-07-27 change (the tilde version was the old `lag_2_w_eps`). The first version of the `1471` PNG carried the tilde; the regenerated one uses $\mathcal W_{it-2}$.
- **Reproduction:** re-fitting `estimate_prod_fn_bounds` on the stored stage-1 fits (`fs_all_ls`, 931.1; "paper" spec) reproduces `PF_me_tbl` (trim) for all 20 cells (5 industries × 4 instruments) to within 5e-5 (table rounding). So the table itself is reproducible; the earlier gap was mine.
- **Where my numbers differed:** my first `1471` run used a "common sample" (rows where both the gross and net shares are defined), dropping 1–12 rows per industry (28 across the five: 313: 1, 331: 2, 322: 6, 369: 7, 321: 12). Even the GROSS spec on that sample differs from the paper in a few cells: 321/$\mathcal W_{it-2}$ $\hat\alpha_K$ 0.372→0.819, 322/$\mathcal W_{it-2}$ 0.177→0.880, 369/$m^*_{it-1}$ $\hat\alpha_K$ 0.331→0.271 and $\hat\alpha_L$ 0.606→0.756, 321/$l_{it-1}$ 0→0.183. Everything else (β̂ changes ≤0.004; $\hat\alpha_L$; most $\hat\alpha_K$) is within ~0.01. **Mechanism tested (jackknife, 40 replicates dropping the same number of RANDOM rows from the paper's own sample):** 321/$\mathcal W_{it-2}$ flips to $\hat\alpha_K\approx0.92$–0.97 in 25% of replicates (two basins: 0.37 and ~0.92) — the $\hat\alpha_K$ objective is flat/multimodal, so a handful of rows can flip it; 369/$m^*_{it-1}$ $\hat\alpha_L$ reaches 0.75 in some replicates (matches my 0.756) but $\hat\alpha_K$ stays within [0.28,0.34]; 322/$\mathcal W_{it-2}$ never flips under random drops (0/40), so that cell is driven by WHICH rows my filter drops, not by fragility alone (not resolved); 313/$m^*_{it-1}$ and 321/$l_{it-1}$ stable. A further mechanical channel (unverified): `obj_fun_ivar1_bounds` drops non-finite rows BEFORE `lag(..., order_by=year)`, which lags by row within plant, so any removed row silently shifts lags (t−1 becomes t−2) — dropping rows is therefore not innocuous for the IV step.
- **Non-convergence is common even in the paper's spec:** `estimate_prod_fn_bounds` returns convergence code ≠0 (L-BFGS-B, code 52) in 10 of 116 cells (29 industries × 4 instruments) for the paper spec, 7 gross-common, 10 net-common; e.g. 369/$k_{it-1}$ in the paper table. $\hat\alpha_K$ at the bounds 0 or 1 is frequent with $k_{it-1}$/$l_{it-1}$ (e.g. 313/$l_{it-1}$: 1; 331/$l_{it-1}$ and 369/$l_{it-1}$: 0).
- **Consequence for the net-of-tax comparison:** with the fragility above, gross-vs-net differences in $\hat\alpha_K$ in unstable cells are NOT evidence of a tax effect. Cleaner design (not done): keep the paper's sample exactly and impute nothing — e.g. use the net share only where defined and keep the gross share elsewhere (85 rows), or make the lag structure year-based so dropped rows can't shift lags; then compare. $\hat\beta,\hat\alpha_L$ conclusions (tiny changes) are unaffected. `1471-net-tax-pf-params.R` now runs three specs (paper/gross-common/net-common) × four instruments; PNG `Paper/tbls/1471-net-pf-params-table.png` shows all three for the two downstream instruments.

## 2026-09-21 (later still) — Instrument comparison for the PF step, tilded vs untilded (`1472-pf-instrument-comparison.R`, `Code/Products/1472-*`)

- **Setup:** paper's own sample (fs_all_ls, 931.1), 29 industries × 5 instruments: $k_{it-1}$, $l_{it-1}$, $m^*_{it-1}$, $\mathcal W_{it-2}$ (`lag_2_cal_W`, UNtilded: includes $\alpha_Kk+\alpha_Ll$), and $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$ (`lag_2_w_eps`, tilded: $\mathcal W-a_Kk-a_Ll$ at the candidate $\alpha$, a generated instrument). Diagnostics are the `ivreg` ones already returned (weak-instrument F, Wu–Hausman; Sargan is NA — one instrument, one endogenous regressor, exactly identified).
- **Practical metrics (29 industries):** non-converged / $\hat\alpha$ at a bound (0 or 1) / share of cells with $\hat\beta+\hat\alpha_K+\hat\alpha_L\in[0.8,1.2]$: $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$ 1/2/93%; $\mathcal W_{it-2}$ 1/5/90%; $m^*_{it-1}$ 2/3/86%; $k_{it-1}$ 4/1/45%; $l_{it-1}$ 3/10/52%. Median RTS 0.996 ($\tilde{\mathcal W}$), 0.963 ($\mathcal W$), 1.007 ($m^*$), 0.778 ($k$), 0.838 ($l$). Median across-industry sd of $\hat\alpha_K$ over the five instruments: 0.109 ($\hat\alpha_L$: 0.099).
- **Stability under random row drops** (40 reps, 321/322/369, same number of rows as the common-sample filter dropped): $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$, $m^*_{it-1}$ and $k_{it-1}$ are stable (α̂_K ranges e.g. 0.30–0.31, 0.32–0.33, 0.06–0.07 for 321); $\mathcal W_{it-2}$ and $l_{it-1}$ are fragile (321/$\mathcal W_{it-2}$: [0.36, 1.00], flips >0.1 in 17.5% of reps; 322/$\mathcal W_{it-2}$: [0.17, 0.86]; 369/$l_{it-1}$: [0, 0.33], 20%). So the earlier $\hat\alpha_K$ jumps under the common-sample filter are an untilded-instrument fragility, not a tax effect.
- **Agreement:** $m^*_{it-1}$ and $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$ give close $(\hat\alpha_K,\hat\alpha_L)$ in 4/5 paper industries (e.g. 322: 0.161/0.387 vs 0.178/0.390; 369: 0.331/0.606 vs 0.334/0.548); 313 differs ($\hat\alpha_K$ 0.449 vs 0 at the bound). Untilded $\mathcal W_{it-2}$ differs from both in 321 and 369.
- **Caveats:** the reported first-stage F are non-clustered, in the hundreds to tens of thousands (tilded largest, 2,844 median): uninformative as a strength measure, and mechanically inflated for the tilded instrument since both it and the regressor are built from the same candidate $\alpha$. Wu–Hausman does not reject in a few cells (321/$\mathcal W_{it-2}$ p=0.34; 369/$m^*_{it-1}$ p=0.52 with F=16.7 — weak there). No formal instrument-validity test has been run yet (proposed: plant-clustered first-stage/effective F and Anderson–Rubin CI; joint-instrument Hansen J and difference-in-Sargan; cross-instrument Hausman with a plant bootstrap; extra-moment J for the outer $(\alpha_K,\alpha_L)$ GMM).

## 2026-09-21 (evening) — PF-step test inversion over $(\alpha_K,\alpha_L)$ by instrument (`1473-pf-testinv-regions.R`, `Code/Products/1473-*`, `Paper/images/1473-pf-testinv-regions.png`)

- **Decisions (user):** drop the untilded $\mathcal W_{it-2}$; keep $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$ (tilded) and $m^*_{it-1}$ as the leading candidates, bring $k_{it-1}$/$l_{it-1}$ back as a check on agreement. Not testing $k,l$ timing or $\beta$ (β treated as known at its stage-1 value, so the test is conditional on $\hat\beta$). Reasoning kept: with ONE instrument the inner step is exactly identified, so an inner-only J is identically zero and validity is untestable; the informative object is a confidence region for $\alpha$ per instrument and how the regions overlap. Agreement does not validate (all lagged instruments share the same identifying assumption) — it only flags instrument-specific violations, and picking the agreeing cluster needs a plurality of valid instruments.
- **Method:** at each grid point in $[0,1]^2$ (step 0.02), $w=\mathcal W-\alpha_Kk-\alpha_Ll$, inner AR(1) $w_t=\gamma_0+\gamma_1w_{t-1}+\eta_t$; four moments $(1,Z,k,l)\eta$; $(\gamma_0,\gamma_1)$ profiled in closed form (moments linear in $\gamma$); efficient two-step GMM with plant-clustered, centered covariance; $J=n\bar r'W\bar r$; region = $J\le\chi^2_{2,.95}=5.99$ (sharp, strong identification of $\gamma$), light = conservative $\chi^2_{4,.95}=9.49$. Paper's own sample; lags row-based within plant (as in `obj_fun_ivar1_bounds`).
- **Result (share of the $[0,1]^2$ grid inside the sharp region; min J≈0 everywhere, as expected when exactly identified):** $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$ is the tightest region in 4/5 paper industries (331: 4.9%, 321: 2.1%, 322: 1.8%, 313: 9%, 369: 7.5%); $m^*_{it-1}$: 22.5%, 2.8%, 1.5%, 28.9%, 48.4%; $k_{it-1}$: 8.5%, 4.0%, 1.9%, 4.0%, 12.2%; $l_{it-1}$: 17.2%, 11.5%, 7.0%, 21.3%, 33.3% (its regions are horizontal bands: it pins $\alpha_L$, not $\alpha_K$). Pairwise overlap $\tilde{\mathcal W}\cap m^*$: 331 3.3%, 321 1.7%, 322 1.5%, 313 6.0%, 369 5.8% — non-empty, and in 321/322/369 a tight ellipse around both point estimates; all-four intersection ≈0 in 4/5 (331: 0.6%, 369: 0.2%). $k_{it-1}$ pulls $\hat\alpha_K$ toward 0 in 321/322/369 and disagrees with the $\tilde{\mathcal W}$–$m^*$ cluster; in 331 $\tilde{\mathcal W}$, $m^*$ and $k$ agree near $(\alpha_K,\alpha_L)\approx(0.05\text{–}0.15,\,0.55\text{–}0.6)$; 313 is not resolved ($\tilde{\mathcal W}$'s optimizer estimate sits at the $\alpha_K=0$ corner while its region extends to $\alpha_K\approx0.25\text{–}0.6$, flat). Regions are elongated along a negative $\alpha_K$–$\alpha_L$ trade-off.
- **Caveats:** the optimizer's point estimates use an identity weight with bounds while the regions use the efficient weight, so a cross need not sit at the region's efficient-GMM solution (corners in 313); grid step 0.02; χ² calibration under profiling is asymptotic; no first-stage/β sampling correction.

## 2026-09-21 (night) — Joint efficient-GMM refit of the PF step (m*_{it-1} + W~_{it-2} together), identity vs efficient weight (`1474-pf-joint-efficient-gmm.R`, `Code/Products/1474-pf-joint-gmm.csv`)

- **Design (answering "how would we do the efficient weight matrix with the embedded 2SLS"):** for FIXED $(\alpha_K,\alpha_L)$, $w_t=\mathcal W_t-\alpha_Kk_t-\alpha_Ll_t$ is data, so $\eta=w_t-\gamma_0-\gamma_1w_{t-1}$ is linear in $(\gamma_0,\gamma_1)$ for any weight $W$: closed-form weighted-2SLS/GMM-IV $\hat\gamma(\alpha,W)=[B(\alpha)'WB(\alpha)]^{-1}B(\alpha)'Wa(\alpha)$ — the outer numerical search stays over $(\alpha_K,\alpha_L)$ only, exactly as the current bounded L-BFGS-B optimizer. 5 moments $(1,m^*_{it-1},\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2},k,l)\cdot\eta$ (BOTH instruments jointly, not one-at-a-time as in `1472`/`1473`), 4 parameters, 1 overidentifying restriction. **Step 1 ("identity-like"):** $W=\text{diag}(1/\text{var}(\text{moment}_j))$ computed once at the OLS start (fixed before the search — a literal untransformed identity is meaningless here since the 5 moments have very different raw scales, e.g. an intercept moment vs. $k\cdot\eta$). **Step 2 (efficient):** plant-clustered $\hat\Omega$ of the moments at the step-1 optimum, $W=\hat\Omega^{-1}$, re-optimize (standard two-step efficient GMM). 3 multi-starts (OLS, $(0.3,0.3)$, $(0.1,0.5)$) + 2-pass L-BFGS-B refinement per start, same "same-point/multi-start beats chaining" convention used elsewhere in this project.
- **Sample:** paper's own data (`fs_all_ls`, 931.1), but now requires BOTH lags ($t-1$ and $t-2$) since $\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2}$ needs two — smaller $n$ than either single-instrument fit (e.g. 322: 2,951 vs. 4,320 with $m^*$ alone).
- **Result:** identity-step vs. efficient-step estimates move by a small-to-moderate amount, mean $|\Delta\hat\alpha_K|=0.067$, mean $|\Delta\hat\alpha_L|=0.028$ across the 5 industries. Largest move: 313 ($\hat\alpha_K$ 0.413→0.243, $\Delta=-0.17$; $\hat\alpha_L$ 0.27→0.159); smallest: 322 ($\hat\alpha_L$ unchanged at 0.373). **Overidentification test (J_eff vs. $\chi^2_{1,.95}=3.84$): all 5 industries PASS (don't reject that both instruments are jointly consistent with the moments)** — J ranges 0.12 (331) to 2.83 (369). The joint-GMM estimates land close to, but not identical to, either single-instrument 2SLS number (e.g. 322: joint efficient $\hat\alpha_K=0.138$ vs. single-instrument $m^*$=0.161, $\tilde{\mathcal W}$=0.178; 313: joint efficient $\hat\alpha_K=0.243$, between the single-instrument $m^*$=0.449 and $\tilde{\mathcal W}$=0 corner).
- **Not done:** standard errors (delta-method sandwich for the efficient-GMM estimator, or a plant bootstrap); a third (CUE/iterated) step; extending beyond the 5 paper industries; a formal comparison of $\hat\alpha_K,\hat\alpha_L,\hat\beta$'s downstream effect on $f_u$/stage 2.

## 2026-09-21 (later still) — PF step, industry 313 fails the sharp joint test; NOT a weak-identification issue — deferred, not resolved

- **Test-inversion (1477):** joint 5-moment efficient-GMM system (1, m*_{it-1}, W~_{it-2}, k, l)*eta, grid over $(\alpha_K,\alpha_L)\in[0,1]^2$, $\beta$ fixed at its stage-1 value, $(\gamma_0,\gamma_1)$ profiled per cell. 4/5 paper industries (331, 322, 369, 321) have a non-empty, bounded region at the sharp level ($\chi^2_{3,.95}=7.81$). **313's sharp region is EMPTY — min $J=9.74$ at $(\alpha_K,\alpha_L)=(0.10,0.18)$, still above 7.81.** Passes the conservative test ($\chi^2_{5,.95}=11.07$; region $\alpha_K\in[0,0.28],\alpha_L\in[0.10,0.22]$, 3.2% of the grid).
- **Weak-identification check (1478), ruled out:** hypothesized the sharp test might be miscalibrated because $\gamma$ (the inner AR(1) step) is weakly identified in 313. Checked directly: first-stage F of $w_{it-1}$ on $(m^*_{it-1},\tilde{\mathcal W}_{it-2},k,l)$ is 5,729 at 313's grid minimum (vs. 5,107 for 322, a well-behaved benchmark), $R^2=0.964$ (vs. 0.874); across 313's whole conservative-passing region (84 cells) F stays in the thousands (median 5,426, min 2,783), $R^2\ge0.93$ everywhere. cond($B'WB$) is $\sim$4$\times$ 322's (13,145 vs 3,063) but not in a range signaling numerical fragility. **Conclusion: $\gamma$ is strongly, not weakly, identified in 313 — the empty sharp region is a genuine misfit of the joint moment system for this industry, not a profiling/identification artifact.** The conservative test's pass is therefore a power argument (no credit for profiling → harder to reject anything), not evidence the sharp rejection is spurious.
- **Decision (user, 2026-09-21): leave 313 as-is for now, decide later.** Not investigated further this session (e.g., whether a different instrument combination, a corner-solution issue specific to 313, or genuine model misspecification for this industry is the cause). The updated summary table (`Code/Products/1478-pf-joint-testinv-summary-cons.csv`) reports both the sharp and conservative regions for all 5 industries and is the one to carry into the paper's PF table, with 313 flagged/caveated there.

## 2026-09-22 — Reproducibility: `Code/MANIFEST.md`, a static dependency scanner

- **`Code/000-manifest.R`** (re-runnable, `Rscript Code/000-manifest.R`): best-effort regex scan of every `Code/**/*.R` (`load`/`save`/`readRDS`/`saveRDS`/`read.csv`/`write.csv`/`ggsave`/`render_png_tt_tbl`/`render_png_etbl`/`source`) plus `Paper|Quarto-Slides|Thesis/**/*.qmd` (markdown image refs), builds a producer/consumer graph, writes `Code/MANIFEST.md`. Flags (a) broken loads (a script depends on a file nothing produces and that isn't on disk — the exact bug hit twice this session: `1475` loading an `.RData` `1474` never saved; `930.1-fs-se-het.R`'s `wip_df` silently inherited from an unrelated script's save); (b) unreferenced outputs (informational). For every `Code/Thesis/*.R` script, prints its transitive upstream through the legacy tree — port traceability with no manual annotation needed, falls out of the same graph.
- **Scope (user, 2026-09-22):** `Code/Ecuador/` and `Code/Spain/` excluded entirely (unrelated projects living in the same `Code/` folder). Files whose basename starts with `_` excluded entirely (this project's own "deprecated" convention). Active code: `Deconvolution`, `Rcpp`, `C-estimator`, `Thesis`. `Colombia` and `Stata` kept in scope (Colombia data wrangling is live; some Stata files may still be used) — `.do` files aren't scanned yet (R-only regexes).
- **Validated on a real bug, unprompted:** `Code/Deconvolution/930.2-boot-se-het.R` loads `Code/Products/930.1-fs-se-het.RData`; the actual producing script (`930.1-fs-se-het.R`) saves to `931.1-fs-se-het.RData` — a naming drift, so `930.2` is currently broken. **Decision (user): leave as-is, documented in the manifest is enough, no fix needed now.**
- **Result after scoping: 1 broken load (930.2, above), 116 unreferenced outputs (informational, not chased down).** `Code/Thesis/ch06-pf-comparison.R` and `ch06-pf-testinv-regions.R`'s upstream traces both resolve cleanly back through `1476`–`1478` → `930.1-fs-se-het.R` → `021-deconv-funs.R` → `Code/Colombia/10_data_wrangling.R`.
- **Known gaps (documented in the manifest's own header):** misses `sprintf`/`paste0`-constructed filenames, positional (non-`file=`) `save()` calls; `.do` (Stata) files not scanned.
- **Not yet done:** wiring into a Makefile target; a similar scan/manifest for `Thesis/chapters+appendices` → asset cross-referencing specifically (currently folded into the same unified scan rather than a separate `Thesis/ASSETS.md`).
